\subsection{How to run}\label{tm:suite:phase4-random-kernels:how-to-run}

\begin{lstlisting}[language=bash]
# From repo root, inside the LibreLane nix-shell:
make -C sim gpu_random                         # 1000 kernels (default)
make -C sim gpu_random_smoke                   # 25-kernel smoke (also runs inside gpu_all)
RANDOM_KERNELS=5000 make -C sim gpu_random     # custom count
RANDOM_KERNELS_SEED=0x1234 make -C sim gpu_random  # different base seed
RANDOM_KERNELS_SEED=0xBAD0 RANDOM_KERNELS=1 make -C sim gpu_random  # reproduce a failing seed

# Full rollup (unit + kernels + handoff + random smoke):
make -C sim gpu_all

# Nightly regression (CPU + cache + GPU + CPU/GPU random):
make -C sim regression_full
\end{lstlisting}

\code{gpu_random} sets \texttt{EXTRA\_\allowbreak{}ARGS=\allowbreak{}""}
to disable Verilator \code{--trace}, so no multi-GB \code{dump.vcd}.

\subsection{Methodology}\label{tm:suite:phase4-random-kernels:methodology}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{DUT:} \code{gpu_top} (full GPU: command queue, scheduler,
  compute unit, regfile, ALU, memory unit + coalescer, shared memory).
\item
  \textbf{Reference model:} \src{tb/cocotb/gpu/gpu_ref_model.py}
  (\code{GpuRefModel}), a Python interpreter that matches the actual
  RTL/\code{gpu_asm} opcode encoding (\code{gpu_pkg.sv}) and
  \code{vector_alu.sv} semantics exactly (VMUL lower-32, VSRL logical,
  VSRA arithmetic, sign-extended 12-bit immediates, r0 hardwired). As of
  2026-06-01 the model also implements:

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Single-level SIMT divergence stack (\code{VBEQ/VBNE/VBLT/VBGE},
    \code{VJMP}): per-lane active mask, taken/\allowbreak{}not-taken
    split, reconvergence via stack pop on VRET. Semantics validated
    against \code{test_divergence.py} and the \code{gpu_compute_unit.sv}
    divergence stack contract.
  \item
    \code{initial_gmem} parameter: pre-seeds global memory before
    execution so VLD reads return deterministic values. The model
    returns \code{gmem_writes} (VST destinations only), not the
    pre-seeded read-only input entries.
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Generator} (\code{test_random_kernels.py}, seeded per kernel
  for reproducibility): each kernel is a single 8-lane warp with these
  building blocks applied probabilistically:
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{Kernel building blocks}\label{tm:suite:phase4-random-kernels:kernel-building-blocks}

{\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*312/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*116/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*552/1000\relax}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Block
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Probability
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
What it exercises
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
Straight-line ALU + global stores & always & ALU R/I ops, regfile,
global store (memory unit + coalescer + AXI), VSYNC (NOP) \\
\textbf{B1 global loads} & 40\% & VLD from \code{INPUT_REGION} (0x100),
address calculation, feed loaded values into compute \\
\textbf{B2 shared memory} & 40\% & VSTS → VSYNC barrier → VLDS
round-trip; smem bank addressing; feed back into compute + VST \\
\textbf{B3 divergence diamond} & 35\% & VBLT with per-lane signed
compare, single-level SIMT push/pop, masked VST on each path \\
\end{longtable}
}

}

A single kernel may receive 0, 1, 2, or all 3 extensions simultaneously.

\subsubsection{Address layout}\label{tm:suite:phase4-random-kernels:address-layout}

\begin{textblock}
Global address space:
0x000–0x0FF : (unused — VRET fallback returns 0x3F from instr_responder)
0x100–0x11F : INPUT_REGION — 8 words (one per lane); pre-seeded; read-only VLD source
0x200–0x23F : STORE_REGIONS[0] — baseline store region (also B3 unreachable-tail store)
0x240–0x27F : STORE_REGIONS[1] — baseline store, or B3 false-path diamond store
0x280–0x2BF : STORE_REGIONS[2] — baseline store, or B3 true-path diamond store

Shared address space (separate from global):
0x000–0x1FF : SMEM (shared scratchpad) — 512 B window used (physical 16 KB / 32
              banks); lane writes smem[lane*4]
\end{textblock}

Regions are non-overlapping. Store lane stride is 4 bytes; 8 lanes = 32
bytes per region, well within the 64-byte region spacing.

\subsubsection{Scoreboard}\label{tm:suite:phase4-random-kernels:scoreboard}

After each kernel:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \code{gpu_wait_done} (timeout=5000 cycles) detects deadlocks.
\item
  \code{data_mem} (AXI responder dict) is compared against
  \texttt{expected\ =\allowbreak{}\ GpuRefModel(.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}).\allowbreak{}run(words)}:

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \code{expected} = \code{gmem_writes} only (VST destinations; no
    pre-seeded read entries).
  \item
    \code{rtl_stores} = \code{data_mem} minus \code{input_gmem}
    addresses (pre-seeded reads are not RTL writes).
  \item
    Both sets must have equal cardinality and matching values at every
    address.
  \end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}

\subsubsection{Divergence diamond shape}\label{tm:suite:phase4-random-kernels:divergence-diamond-shape}

The B3 generator always emits a provably-reconverging,
provably-terminating, single-level diamond:

\begin{textblock}
VBLT rs1=working_reg, rs2=r0, target=true_path   ; push(false_path_pc, not_taken_mask)
; fall-through (false-path, lanes where working_reg[l] >= 0):
  VADDI  div_reg, r0, false_val
  VADDI  R_BASE, r0, (STORE_REGIONS[1] - div_reg_idx)
  VADD   R_BASE, R_BASE, R_OFF
  VST    div_reg, R_BASE, 0                        ; mem[STORE_REGIONS[1] + l*4] = false_val
  VRET                                              ; end false-path
; [pad VRETs to align true_path_pc to branch_pc+32]
true_path:
  VADDI  div_reg, r0, true_val
  VADDI  R_BASE, r0, (STORE_REGIONS[2] - div_reg_idx)
  VADD   R_BASE, R_BASE, R_OFF
  VST    div_reg, R_BASE, 0                        ; mem[STORE_REGIONS[2] + l*4] = true_val
  VRET                                              ; pop → execute false-path lanes → VRET
\end{textblock}

Branch offset = 32 bytes (multiple of 32, so \code{rs2=r0} index=0
satisfies the branch encoding constraint
\texttt{offset[4:\allowbreak{}0]\ =\allowbreak{}=\allowbreak{}\ rs2\_\allowbreak{}index}).
Branch condition
\texttt{VBLT\ rs1,\allowbreak{}\ rs2=\allowbreak{}r0,\allowbreak{}\ .\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}}
compares signed \texttt{rs1[l]\ <\ 0}, which splits the 8 lanes based on
the sign of whatever accumulated value is in \code{rs1}. If all lanes
happen to agree (all same sign after the compute phase), the branch
converges --- the model and RTL both handle converging branches
correctly.

\subsection{Scope rationale}\label{tm:suite:phase4-random-kernels:scope-rationale}

{\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-2\tabcolsep)*474/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-2\tabcolsep)*506/1000\relax}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Feature
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Covered by
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
ALU R-type
(VADD/\allowbreak{}VSUB/\allowbreak{}VMUL/\allowbreak{}VAND/\allowbreak{}VOR/\allowbreak{}VXOR/\allowbreak{}VSLL/\allowbreak{}VSRL/\allowbreak{}VSRA)
& random kernel (always) \\
ALU I-type
(VADDI/\allowbreak{}VANDI/\allowbreak{}VORI/\allowbreak{}VXORI) & random
kernel (always) \\
VMOV\_\allowbreak{}TID\_\allowbreak{}X & random kernel (always, init) \\
Global store (VST) via AXI4 + coalescer & random kernel (always) \\
VSYNC (single-warp NOP) & random kernel (always, low probability) \\
Global load (VLD) & B1 (40\% probability per kernel) \\
Shared-memory round-trip (VSTS/\allowbreak{}VLDS) & B2 (40\% probability
per kernel) \\
Single-level divergence (VBLT, SIMT stack) & B3 (35\% probability per
kernel) \\
Multi-level divergence, loops, backward branches & directed tests only
(\code{kernel_divergence_basic}, \code{test_divergence.py}) ---
intentionally excluded from random generator \\
Multi-warp scheduling & directed tests (\code{test_warp_scheduler.py},
\code{kernel_sync}) \\
\end{longtable}
}

}

Restricting the random space to the exactly-modeled subset keeps every
mismatch a genuine RTL datapath bug rather than a
generator/\allowbreak{}model artifact, while the 1000-seed campaign
exercises broad
ALU/\allowbreak{}regfile/\allowbreak{}load/\allowbreak{}store/\allowbreak{}smem/\allowbreak{}divergence
combinations.

\subsection{Reproducing a specific seed}\label{tm:suite:phase4-random-kernels:reproducing-a-specific-seed}

\begin{lstlisting}[language=bash]
RANDOM_KERNELS_SEED=0xBAD0 RANDOM_KERNELS=1 make -C sim gpu_random
\end{lstlisting}

The first 10 failing seeds are also logged as \code{ERROR} lines in the
cocotb output, so reproduction does not require external tracking.
\end{relocated}

\begin{relocated}{docs/development/CONTROL_SIGNALS_ANALYSIS.md}
% Converted from docs/development/CONTROL_SIGNALS_ANALYSIS.md by tools/docs/md2tex.py; edit this file, not the original.
\section{\texorpdfstring{Control Signal Analysis for
rv32i\_\allowbreak{}decode.sv}{Control Signal Analysis for rv32i\_decode.sv}}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis}

\subsection{Overview}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:overview}

This document analyzes additional control signals that may be needed for
the RV32I decoder beyond what\textquotesingle s in the EXAMPLE decoder.

\subsection{Currently Proposed Signals (in EXAMPLE)}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:currently-proposed-signals-in-example}

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}ccc@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Signal & Width & Purpose \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\code{rs1}, \code{rs2}, \code{rd} & 5 bits each & Register addresses \\
\code{alu_op} & 4 bits & ALU operation selection \\
\code{alu_src_a} & 1 bit & ALU input A mux (rs1 vs PC) \\
\code{alu_src_b} & 1 bit & ALU input B mux (rs2 vs imm) \\
\code{imm_fmt} & 3 bits & Immediate format for generator \\
\code{reg_wr_en} & 1 bit & Register write enable \\
\code{mem_rd}, \code{mem_wr} & 1 bit each & Memory
read/\allowbreak{}write enables \\
\code{mem_size} & 3 bits & Memory access size \\
\code{mem_unsigned} & 1 bit & Unsigned load flag \\
\code{branch} & 1 bit & Branch instruction flag \\
\code{branch_op} & 3 bits & Branch operation type \\
\code{jump} & 1 bit & Jump instruction flag \\
\code{jalr} & 1 bit & JALR vs JAL \\
\code{illegal} & 1 bit & Illegal instruction flag \\
\end{longtable}
}

\subsection{Potentially Missing Signals}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:potentially-missing-signals}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\code{mem_to_reg} (Write-Back Mux
Control)}{(Write-Back Mux Control)}}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:1-mem-to-reg-write-back-mux-control}\origsec{1}

\textbf{Purpose}: Selects the source for register writeback value

\textbf{Encoding} (proposed):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{2'b00} = ALU result
\item
  \texttt{2'b01} = Memory read data (for loads)
\item
  \texttt{2'b10} = PC + 4 (for JAL/JALR)
\item
  \texttt{2'b11} = Immediate (for LUI - may not be needed if ALU can
  pass through)
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Importance}: \textbf{*}\textbf{*}\textbf{*} \textbf{CRITICAL}

\textbf{Why it\textquotesingle s critical}:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Load instructions need to write memory data to register, not ALU
  result
\item
  JAL/JALR need to write PC+4 to link register
\item
  Without this, all register writes would get ALU result
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Example usage}:

\begin{lstlisting}[language=Verilog]
// In writeback stage
always_comb begin
  case (mem_to_reg)
    2'b00: reg_wr_data = alu_result;
    2'b01: reg_wr_data = mem_read_data;
    2'b10: reg_wr_data = pc_plus_4;
    2'b11: reg_wr_data = immediate;
  endcase
end
\end{lstlisting}

\textbf{Instructions that need it}:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{mem\_\allowbreak{}to\_\allowbreak{}reg\ =\allowbreak{}\ 2'b01}:
  LB, LH, LW, LBU, LHU (all loads)
\item
  \texttt{mem\_\allowbreak{}to\_\allowbreak{}reg\ =\allowbreak{}\ 2'b10}:
  JAL, JALR
\item
  \texttt{mem\_\allowbreak{}to\_\allowbreak{}reg\ =\allowbreak{}\ 2'b00}:
  All ALU operations (default)
\item
  \texttt{mem\_\allowbreak{}to\_\allowbreak{}reg\ =\allowbreak{}\ 2'b11}:
  LUI (if not using ALU passthrough)
\end{itemize}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\code{pc_wr_en} (Program Counter Write
Enable)}{(Program Counter Write Enable)}}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:2-pc-wr-en-program-counter-write-enable}\origsec{2}

\textbf{Purpose}: Controls when the PC register is updated

\textbf{Encoding}:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{1'b0} = Hold PC (stall)
\item
  \texttt{1'b1} = Update PC
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Importance}: \textbf{*}\textbf{*} \textbf{IMPORTANT} (but may be
handled by control FSM instead)

\textbf{Why it matters}:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Needed to stall PC during AXI memory transactions
\item
  Prevents PC from incrementing during multi-cycle operations
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Alternative approach}: The control FSM
(rv32i\_\allowbreak{}control.sv) could directly manage PC updates based
on FSM state, making this decoder signal unnecessary.

\textbf{Recommendation}: \bad{} \textbf{NOT needed in decoder} - This is
better handled by the control FSM which knows about AXI stalls and
pipeline state.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\code{pc_src} (Program Counter Source
Mux)}{(Program Counter Source Mux)}}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:3-pc-src-program-counter-source-mux}\origsec{3}

\textbf{Purpose}: Selects the next PC value

\textbf{Encoding} (proposed):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{2'b00} = PC + 4 (sequential)
\item
  \texttt{2'b01} = PC + immediate (branches, JAL)
\item
  \texttt{2'b10} = rs1 + immediate (JALR)
\item
  \texttt{2'b11} = TRAP\_\allowbreak{}VECTOR (traps)
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Importance}: \textbf{*}\textbf{*}\textbf{*} \textbf{CRITICAL}

\textbf{Why it\textquotesingle s critical}:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Determines control flow for branches and jumps
\item
  Required for implementing branch/\allowbreak{}jump instructions
  correctly
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Note}: The EXAMPLE decoder has \code{pc_src} as 1-bit, but needs
to be expanded to 2 bits.

\textbf{Instructions that need it}:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{pc\_\allowbreak{}src\ =\allowbreak{}\ 2'b00}: All
  non-control-flow instructions
\item
  \texttt{pc\_\allowbreak{}src\ =\allowbreak{}\ 2'b01}: JAL, taken
  branches (BEQ, BNE, BLT, BGE, BLTU, BGEU)
\item
  \texttt{pc\_\allowbreak{}src\ =\allowbreak{}\ 2'b10}: JALR
\item
  \texttt{pc\_\allowbreak{}src\ =\allowbreak{}\ 2'b11}: Illegal
  instruction trap (set by control FSM, not decoder)
\end{itemize}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\code{result_src} or \code{wb_src}
(Alternative name for
mem\_\allowbreak{}to\_\allowbreak{}reg)}{or  (Alternative name for mem\_to\_reg)}}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:4-result-src-or-wb-src-alternative-name-for-mem-to-reg}\origsec{4}

\textbf{Same as \code{mem_to_reg}} - just different naming conventions.

Some designs use:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \code{mem_to_reg} (emphasizes memory-to-register path)
\item
  \code{result_src} (more generic, emphasizes result selection)
\item
  \code{wb_src} (writeback source)
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Recommendation}: Use \code{mem_to_reg} as it\textquotesingle s
more descriptive.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\code{alu_zero} (ALU Zero
Output)}{(ALU Zero Output)}}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:5-alu-zero-alu-zero-output}\origsec{5}

\textbf{Purpose}: Flag indicating ALU result is zero

\textbf{Importance}: \bad{} \textbf{NOT needed} as separate decoder
signal

\textbf{Why}:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  This is generated by the ALU itself, not the decoder
\item
  Used internally for some branch implementations
\item
  Since we have a separate branch comparator, not needed
\end{itemize}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\code{trap} or \code{exception}
(Trap/\allowbreak{}Exception Flag)}{or  (Trap/Exception Flag)}}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:6-trap-or-exception-trapexception-flag}\origsec{6}

\textbf{Purpose}: Indicates instruction causes a trap

\textbf{Importance}: \textbf{*}\textbf{*} \textbf{IMPORTANT}

\textbf{Overlap with existing signals}: The EXAMPLE decoder already has
\code{illegal} flag. This covers the main trap case in Phase 1.

\textbf{Additional trap sources} (may need signals):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Misaligned memory access (could be checked by control logic)
\item
  AXI error response (detected during memory stage, not decode)
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Recommendation}: \ok{} Current \code{illegal} signal is
sufficient for Phase 1. Control FSM handles other trap conditions.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsection{Summary and Recommendations}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:summary-and-recommendations}

\subsubsection{Must Add (Critical)}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:must-add-critical}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \ok{} \textbf{\code{mem_to_reg[1:0]}} - MUST add to decoder

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Without this, loads and jumps cannot write correct data to registers
  \item
    Affects: LB, LH, LW, LBU, LHU, JAL, JALR (12 instructions)
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \ok{} \textbf{\code{pc_src[1:0]}} - MUST expand from 1-bit to 2-bits

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Need to distinguish PC+4, PC+imm, rs1+imm
  \item
    Affects: JAL, JALR, all 6 branches (8 instructions)
  \end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}

\subsubsection{Should NOT Add (Handled Elsewhere)}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:should-not-add-handled-elsewhere}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \bad{} \textbf{\code{pc_wr_en}} - Control FSM handles this based on
  state
\item
  \bad{} \textbf{\code{alu_zero}} - Generated by ALU or branch
  comparator
\item
  \bad{} \textbf{Additional trap flags} - \code{illegal} covers Phase 1
  needs
\end{enumerate}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsection{Updated Decoder Interface Proposal}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:updated-decoder-interface-proposal}

\begin{textblock}
module rv32i_decode (
  // Instruction input
  input  logic [31:0] instruction,

  // Decoded register addresses
  output logic [4:0]  rs1,
  output logic [4:0]  rs2,
  output logic [4:0]  rd,

  // ALU control
  output logic [3:0]  alu_op,
  output logic        alu_src_a,    // 0=rs1, 1=PC
  output logic        alu_src_b,    // 0=rs2, 1=immediate

  // Immediate format
  output logic [2:0]  imm_fmt,

  // Register file control
  output logic        reg_wr_en,

  // Memory control
  output logic        mem_rd,
  output logic        mem_wr,
  output logic [2:0]  mem_size,
  output logic        mem_unsigned,

  // Branch/Jump control
  output logic        branch,
  output logic [2:0]  branch_op,
  output logic        jump,
  output logic        jalr,

  // Writeback and PC control
  output logic [1:0]  mem_to_reg,   // ⭐ NEW: 00=ALU, 01=MEM, 10=PC+4, 11=IMM
  output logic [1:0]  pc_src,       // ⭐ EXPANDED: 00=PC+4, 01=PC+imm, 10=rs1+imm, 11=trap

  // Exception
  output logic        illegal
);
\end{textblock}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsection{Instruction-by-Instruction Control Signal Table}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:instruction-by-instruction-control-signal-table}

{\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\centering\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)*450/1000\relax}
  >{\centering\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)*160/1000\relax}
  >{\centering\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)*174/1000\relax}
  >{\centering\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)*196/1000\relax}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\centering
Instruction
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\centering
mem\_\allowbreak{}to\_\allowbreak{}reg
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\centering
pc\_src
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\centering
Notes
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\textbf{ALU ops} (ADD, SUB, AND, OR, XOR, SLL, SRL, SRA, SLT, SLTU) & 00
(ALU) & 00 (PC+4) & Standard ALU operation \\
\textbf{ALU-imm} (ADDI, ANDI, ORI, XORI, SLLI, SRLI, SRAI, SLTI, SLTIU)
& 00 (ALU) & 00 (PC+4) & Standard ALU operation \\
\textbf{Loads} (LB, LH, LW, LBU, LHU) & 01 (MEM) & 00 (PC+4) & Write
memory data to rd \\
\textbf{Stores} (SB, SH, SW) & XX (no wr) & 00 (PC+4) & No register
write \\
\textbf{Branches} (BEQ, BNE, BLT, BGE, BLTU, BGEU) & XX (no wr) & 01 if
taken, 00 if not & Conditional PC update \\
\textbf{JAL} & 10 (PC+4) & 01 (PC+imm) & Save return address \\
\textbf{JALR} & 10 (PC+4) & 10 (rs1+imm) & Save return address \\
\textbf{LUI} & 11 (IMM) or 00 (ALU) & 00 (PC+4) & Depends on
implementation \\
\textbf{AUIPC} & 00 (ALU) & 00 (PC+4) & ALU computes PC+imm \\
\end{longtable}
}

}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsection{Questions for Human Review}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:questions-for-human-review}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{LUI implementation}: Should LUI use
  \texttt{mem\_\allowbreak{}to\_\allowbreak{}reg\ =\allowbreak{}\ 11}
  (pass immediate directly), or
  \texttt{mem\_\allowbreak{}to\_\allowbreak{}reg\ =\allowbreak{}\ 00}
  (use ALU with special handling)?

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Option A: ALU has passthrough mode when
    alu\_\allowbreak{}src\_\allowbreak{}a=0 and alu\_\allowbreak{}op=ADD
    with rs1=x0
  \item
    Option B: Separate immediate path to writeback mux
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{AUIPC implementation}: Currently uses ALU with
  alu\_\allowbreak{}src\_\allowbreak{}a=PC. Is this acceptable?
\item
  \textbf{Trap handling}: Should
  \texttt{pc\_\allowbreak{}src\ =\allowbreak{}\ 11} be set by decoder
  when \texttt{illegal\ =\allowbreak{}\ 1}, or should control FSM
  override pc\_src when handling traps?
\item
  \textbf{Branch not-taken}: When branch is not taken, should decoder
  output \texttt{pc\_\allowbreak{}src\ =\allowbreak{}\ 00}, or should
  this be decided by control logic combining \code{branch} and
  \code{branch_taken} signals?
\end{enumerate}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsection{Recommendation}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:recommendation}

\textbf{Add these two signals to rv32i\_\allowbreak{}decode.sv}:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \code{mem_to_reg[1:0]} - Critical for loads and jumps
\item
  Expand \code{pc_src} to \code{pc_src[1:0]} - Critical for JALR
\end{enumerate}

Without these, the CPU cannot correctly execute 12 instructions (5 loads
+ JAL + JALR + potentially LUI).
\end{relocated}

\chapter{Coverage}\label{tm:ch:coverage}

This chapter describes how the project measures coverage.

\begin{relocated}{docs/verification/COMMERCIAL_SIM_COVERAGE.md}
% Converted from docs/verification/COMMERCIAL_SIM_COVERAGE.md by tools/docs/md2tex.py; edit this file, not the original.
\section{VCS code coverage and hole review (Step 4, report-only)}\label{tm:flow:commercial-sim-coverage}

Environment setup and the uninstrumented VCS/\allowbreak{}Xcelium
regression flow: Doc: um:flow:proprietary-sim.

Measured 2026-10-23/\allowbreak{}24 on branch
\code{feature/commercial-sims}, Synopsys VCS Y-2026.03 + URG Y-2026.03.
Scope decision (user): \textbf{VCS only}. Xcelium coverage was not
collected: \code{imc} is not installed on this host, so a collected
Xcelium database could not be merged or reported. \code{COVERAGE=1}
under \code{SIM=xcelium} is refused with a clear message by
\src{tb/cocotb/common/sim_portability.mk}.

Mode:
\texttt{coverage\_\allowbreak{}mode:\allowbreak{}\ report\_\allowbreak{}only}
(verification-orchestrator Behaviour Rule 14). This pass collects and
reviews. It writes \textbf{no} test, constraint or waiver, and takes
\textbf{no} coverage loop-back (functional \textless{} 100 \% to
\code{constrained_random}, line \textless{} 95 \% to
\code{directed_tests}). Every "proposed test" and "proposed waiver"
below is a proposal for a human to accept or reject. Coverage below
target is a WARN
(\texttt{failure\_\allowbreak{}class:\allowbreak{}\ coverage\_\allowbreak{}gap})
and never sets sign-off.

\subsection{How to run}\label{tm:flow:commercial-sim-coverage:1-how-to-run}\origsec{1}

\begin{lstlisting}[language=bash]
source tools/eda/sim_env.sh                       # VCS_HOME, licence, .venv-eda (python 3.11 + cocotb 1.9.2)

# whole thing: instrumented SoC regression, instrumented CPU/cache/GPU regression, urg merge + report
python3 tools/eda/vcs_coverage.py run --area all --root sim_build_cov --jobs 3

# or per area, through the Makefiles (SIM=vcs only so the soc Makefile parses without a verilator binary)
make -C tb/cocotb/soc SIM=vcs soc_coverage_vcs     # soc_all_ci job list, --jobs $(VCS_COV_JOBS)=3
make -C sim coverage_cpu_gpu_vcs                   # CPU / Phase 3 caches / Phase 4 GPU rollup, serial

# re-merge only (no simulation), e.g. after adding vdbs
python3 tools/eda/vcs_coverage.py merge --root sim_build_cov --out sim_build_cov/report/all

# re-slice a merged report into per-module scores and a machine-readable hole list
python3 tools/eda/vcs_cov_holes.py sim_build_cov/report/all/urgReport --json holes_all.json
\end{lstlisting}

Outputs under \code{sim_build_cov/} (gitignored, not committed):
\texttt{\{soc,\allowbreak{}cpu\}/\allowbreak{}vcs/\allowbreak{}<chain>/\allowbreak{}<build>/\allowbreak{}simv.\allowbreak{}vdb}
(one per build, one \code{-cm_name} test per simulation),
\texttt{report/\allowbreak{}\{soc,\allowbreak{}cpu,\allowbreak{}all\}/\allowbreak{}urgReport/\allowbreak{}\{dashboard,\allowbreak{}modlist,\allowbreak{}hierarchy,\allowbreak{}modinfo,\allowbreak{}tests,\allowbreak{}asserts\}.\allowbreak{}txt}
(+ \code{.html}), \code{report/<area>/urg.log},
\code{report/<area>/merge_summary.json}. \code{modinfo.txt} is the
per-module detail (uncovered lines, branch arms, condition rows, FSM
arcs, untoggled ports).

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{What
\texttt{COVERAGE=\allowbreak{}1\ SIM=\allowbreak{}vcs} does
(tb/\allowbreak{}cocotb/\allowbreak{}common/\allowbreak{}sim\_\allowbreak{}portability.mk)}{What  does (tb/cocotb/common/sim\_portability.mk)}}\label{tm:flow:commercial-sim-coverage:what-coverage1-simvcs-does-tbcocotbcommonsim-portabilitymk}

{\footnotesize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-2\tabcolsep)*274/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-2\tabcolsep)*706/1000\relax}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Aspect
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Implementation
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
Metrics & compile
\texttt{-\allowbreak{}cm\ line+\allowbreak{}cond+\allowbreak{}fsm+\allowbreak{}tgl+\allowbreak{}branch+\allowbreak{}assert};
run with the same \code{-cm} (\code{VCS_COV_METRICS} overrides) \\
Scope & \texttt{-\allowbreak{}cm\_\allowbreak{}hier\ <cfg>} with a
\textbf{leading \code{+filelist}} rule, so everything not listed is
excluded \textbf{at compile time}: the list is the \src{rtl/} files in
this build\textquotesingle s \code{VERILOG_SOURCES} plus the \src{rtl/}
files reachable through the \code{-I}/\code{-y} library lookup.
Testbench wrappers (\code{tb_*}), cocotb glue and the behavioural models
under \src{sim/} are not listed.
\texttt{-\allowbreak{}cm\_\allowbreak{}libs\ yv} so a module found only
through \code{-y} (e.g. \code{rv32i_clock_gate.sv}) is instrumented. The
list is content-addressed (\code{vcs_cov_scope_<hash>.f/.cfg} under
\code{COV_DIR}), so builds with different sources never race on one
file. \src{rtl/fpga/} (the \code{FPGA=1} variant) is not part of these
regressions \\
One database per build & the compile writes the static design into the
build\textquotesingle s own \code{simv.vdb}; every simulation of that
build adds a test to it
(\texttt{-\allowbreak{}cm\_\allowbreak{}dir\ <SIM\_\allowbreak{}BUILD>/\allowbreak{}simv.\allowbreak{}vdb}) \\
Unique test names &
\texttt{-\allowbreak{}cm\_\allowbreak{}name\ <build\ dir>\_\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}<MODULE>[\_\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}tc<TESTCASE\ hash>]\_\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}<hash\ of\ abs\ build\ dir>}.
Suites that share a build dir (the \code{tb_soc_top} chains) therefore
never overwrite each other, and names stay unique when vdbs from
different \code{SIM_BUILD_ROOT}s are merged. Checked: 80 SoC + 34 CPU
\code{results__*.xml} files = 80 + 34 tests recorded in the vdbs = 114 =
urg \texttt{Number\ of\ tests} \\
Separate build dirs & \code{_cov} suffix on every \code{SIM_BUILD}, and
the coverage flags are in the content key, so an instrumented
\code{simv} can never be reused by a plain run (or the reverse) \\
Verilator / Xcelium & the Verilator branch is untouched;
\code{COVERAGE=1} with \code{SIM=xcelium} is an
\texttt{\$(error\ .\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{})}
explaining why \\
Merge & one
\texttt{urg\ -\allowbreak{}lca\ -\allowbreak{}dir\ <all\ vdbs>\ -\allowbreak{}flex\_\allowbreak{}merge\ modules\ -\allowbreak{}dbname\ .\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}\ -\allowbreak{}report\ .\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}\ -\allowbreak{}format\ both}.
\textbf{Why \texttt{-\allowbreak{}flex\_\allowbreak{}merge\ modules}:}
the suites have different top-level testbenches, so the databases have
different base designs; a plain \texttt{urg\ -\allowbreak{}dir\ a\ b}
keeps only the first database\textquotesingle s design and silently
drops every instance it does not contain (observed: merging
\code{tb_cdc_gray_fifo} + \code{tb_axi4_crossbar} produced a report with
no crossbar). \textbf{It is an LCA (limited customer availability) URG
feature and needs \code{-lca}}; if a future VCS drops it, merge per
top-level or use \code{-map} \\
Empty-report guard &
\texttt{vcs\_\allowbreak{}coverage.\allowbreak{}py\ merge} fails if the
dashboard "Total Coverage Summary" row is all \code{--} (nothing
instrumented, or scope excluded everything), and warns if
urg\textquotesingle s \texttt{Number\ of\ tests} differs from the number
recorded in the vdbs \\
\end{longtable}
}

}

Verdicts come only from the \code{results__*.xml} files (cocotb 1.x
\code{make} exits 0 on failure). Wall clock,
\texttt{-\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}jobs\ 3} for SoC and serial for
\src{sim/}: SoC 1090 s (uninstrumented 842 s), CPU 316 s (214 s), urg
merges 5 s + 1 s + 12 s. Total about 24 min instrumented against about
18 min plain (about 1.4x). Disk: \code{sim_build_cov/} 616 MB (SoC 345
MB, CPU 147 MB, reports 125 MB); the 100 \code{simv.vdb} databases
themselves total 20.6 MB; the merged report directories are 56 MB (soc),
6.6 MB (cpu), 61 MB (all) of which most is HTML.

\texttt{make\ -\allowbreak{}n} parity of the Verilator path (stubbed
\code{verilator}, \code{cocotb-config} from the venv): 30 targets (soc,
cpu and \src{sim/}, including \code{COVERAGE=1} on \texttt{soc\ gpio}
and \texttt{sim\ icache}, \code{soc_all_ci}, \code{soc_ci_shard},
\code{print-sim-build}) captured at HEAD before and in the working tree
after; \texttt{cmp\ -\allowbreak{}s} on every pair: \textbf{30 of 30
byte-identical}. The new targets are appended at the end of
\src{tb/cocotb/soc/Makefile} and \src{sim/Makefile}, so no earlier line
number moved.

How to read the two views. The \textbf{module-definition} view merges
all instances of a module (the same "hit if any instance hit it" rule as
\src{tools/verif/coverage_report.py}), and counts every parameter shape
(\texttt{(\ parameter\ .\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}\ )}
variant) as its own definition, so a rarely-used shape that one small
test exercises pulls the total down. The \textbf{instance-hierarchy}
view scores each top-level testbench tree separately: a block seen only
through a boot test inside \code{tb_soc_top} (for example
\code{tb_soc_pll.u_soc.u_pmu} at 50.5 \%, against 83.7 \% for the
module) is scored on that instance alone, which is why it is much lower.
Neither is a sign-off number. The per-module rows below are the
module-level merged scores (top row of each module in
\code{modlist.txt}).

The sharp reading: \textbf{statement and branch coverage are high
(median 100 \%); the gaps are FSM transitions, condition rows and
toggle.} Every FSM \emph{state} is reached (state coverage 100 \%
wherever an FSM was extracted); what is missing are 76 transitions, and
58 of them are "reset asserted while the FSM is busy" arcs (section 3,
H1, H2, H6, H14).

Hole totals (never hit in any shape that lists the point): 69 lines, 61
branch arms, 585 condition rows, 76 FSM arcs, 1 860 never-toggled
port/\allowbreak{}signal rows (rows are bit ranges, not bits). 38 of the
69 lines are \code{default:} arms, and 33 of those already carry a
category-b waiver in \src{tools/verif/coverage_waivers.txt} (anchor-text
match), written for Verilator; \textbf{VCS reads neither that file nor
the 11
\texttt{/\allowbreak{}*\ verilator\ coverage\_\allowbreak{}off\ */\allowbreak{}}
pragmas in \src{rtl/}}, so those points reappear here.

\subsection{Ranked hole list (highest risk first)}\label{tm:flow:commercial-sim-coverage:3-ranked-hole-list-highest-risk-first}\origsec{3}

Ranking key: (1) unvisited FSM states and transitions, (2) error /
\code{default} / DECERR / SLVERR paths, (3) reset, flush and abort
paths, (4) CDC and async FIFO corner cases, (5) PMU timeout, (6) crypto
illegal start, (7) NPU KLEN extremes, (8) DMA error paths, (9) the rest.
Within a class, by consequence of a latent bug and then by size. The
user\textquotesingle s class order is kept, but note one measured fact
that reshapes it: \textbf{all FSM states were reached, and 58 of the 76
missing FSM transitions are the reset arm of the state register}
(\texttt{if\ (!rst\_\allowbreak{}n)\ state\ <=\allowbreak{}\ IDLE},
which VCS counts as an arc from every non-idle state). Classes 1 and 3
therefore overlap; they are listed once, under class 1, and tagged.

Evidence for classes the user asked about, even where the answer is "no
hole":

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{PMU timeout (\code{ISO_ACK_TIMEOUT}, \code{STATUS[3]})}: not a
  hole. \code{pmu.sv} has no uncovered line in the \code{g_ack_cpu} wait
  and timeout block; the timeout path is exercised (the suites written
  for it are \code{test_pmu.py} and \code{test_soc_pmu_iso_ack.py};
  per-test attribution was not extracted). The remaining \code{pmu}
  holes are H6 (reset during sequencing) and the waivable items W5.
\item
  \textbf{Crypto illegal start / \code{key_write_rejected}}: not a
  code-coverage hole. \code{crypto_accel} line 100 \%, branch 100 \%,
  FSM 100 \%; \code{aes128_core} line 100 \%. Condition rows are in
  section 6 (caveat C3).
\item
  \textbf{NPU KLEN extremes}: not visible to code coverage.
  \code{npu_top} line/\allowbreak{}cond/\allowbreak{}branch 100 \%, and
  no KLEN bit is among its never-toggled signals (every bit toggled both
  ways). Whether KLEN = 1 and KLEN = 63 are both run is a
  \emph{functional coverage} question; no covergroup exists for the NPU
  (section 7).
\item
  \textbf{CDC and async FIFO full/\allowbreak{}empty}:
  \code{cdc_gray_fifo} line/\allowbreak{}cond/\allowbreak{}branch 100
  \%, toggle 96.5 \% (the residual are payload bits), so full and empty
  are exercised. \code{async_axi_fifo} has no line/\allowbreak{}branch
  objects (it is structural) and cond 100 \%. The CDC holes are in
  \code{apb_cdc_bridge} (H7).
\item
  \textbf{DMA error paths}: error handling lines are covered
  (\code{dma_engine} line 98.7 \%, only L394 and a \code{default:}
  open). The DMA holes are queue depth and one dead branch (H9).
\end{itemize}

352/\allowbreak{}418/\allowbreak{}475 (12 arcs:

engine"; this is exactly the untested path & \textbf{Test.} One new test
per suite (\code{test_crossbar_errors.py},
\code{test_axil_interconnect.py}, \code{test_axil_to_apb.py},
\code{test_axi4_to_axilite.py}, \code{test_boot_rom.py},
\code{test_sram_controller.py}): start a write burst, drop \code{rst_n}
for 2 cycles while the FSM is in each non-idle state (hold AW
accepted/\allowbreak{}W pending; AR accepted/\allowbreak{}R pending),

transaction completes. Reset \emph{then} check slave-side state, not
just the master view \\

abort a transfer by resetting \code{m_rst_n}, and assert \code{s_rst}

\code{m_rst_n} for a single destination cycle (shorter than the 2-FF
sync) in the middle of a write and expect either the real response or
\code{pslverr=1}, never a hang \\

category b in the Verilator file) \\
Elaboration guards & \code{apb4_register_bank.sv} 61-62,
\code{axi_lite_register_bank.sv} 80-81, \code{axil_to_apb.sv} 93-94,
\code{soc_top.sv} 408 (\texttt{\$fatal} on illegal parameters) & an
elaboration error aborts the build; unreachable in a passing run &
\textbf{Waiver W2} \\
Dead / redundant code & \code{dma_engine.sv} 394 (saturate
\code{beats_raw} to \code{MAX_BURST_BEATS}: \code{beats_raw} is already
\texttt{min2(.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}\ MAX\_\allowbreak{}BURST\_\allowbreak{}BEATS)}
so it never exceeds it); \code{rv32i_icache.sv} 510
(\code{cancel_wait_r_q}: the source comment says "unused in burst mode
but kept for symmetry") & unreachable & \textbf{Waiver W7} or RTL
cleanup \\
PMU defaults and GPU-domain handshake & \code{pmu.sv} 494-498 and B429
\code{default} (output decode of an invalid state),
B409/\allowbreak{}L413/\allowbreak{}L421 (GPU domain \code{d=1}:
\code{iso_ack_w} is tied, so the wait-for-ack arms of
\code{DOM_PD_ISO}/\code{DOM_PU_ISO} are CPU-only), L538 row
\texttt{0\ 1} (counter equals the threshold while not waiting:
impossible) & by design & \textbf{Waiver W5} \\
Tie-offs and constants & \code{irq_src_i[2]} (TIMER slot tied 0 in
\code{soc_top}, "routing it here too would double-count"),
\code{pready}/\code{pslverr} of every APB peripheral (tied 1/0,
zero-wait-state), constant AXI sideband (\code{m_arburst},
\code{m_arsize}, \code{m_awburst}, \code{m_awsize}, \code{m_arid},
\code{m_awid}, \code{m_wstrb}, \code{arprot}, \code{awprot}), spare
address bits (\code{addr_ext_w[31:10]}) & tied or constant &
\textbf{Waiver W3/W4} \\
MBIST / DFT observability & \code{mbist_ctrl.sv} cond 503/521 (per-slot
\code{m0/m1}, \texttt{ev\_\allowbreak{}w\ \&\&\ !fail\_\allowbreak{}q}:
a second failure on an already-failed slot), \code{soc_mbist.sv} cond
158
(\texttt{core\_\allowbreak{}wr\_\allowbreak{}ready\_\allowbreak{}w\ \&\ cpu\_\allowbreak{}wr\_\allowbreak{}ready\_\allowbreak{}w},
one ready low), \code{dft_ctrl.sv}/\code{jtag_tap.sv}
\code{mbist_status_i} bits 0, 3, 16, 28, 31-35, 72, 74-80, 82-83, 86 and
88-93 never toggle & only the pass/done bits of the MBIST status word
ever change in SoC-level runs & \textbf{Test} in
\code{test_mbist_soc_slice.py}/\code{test_mbist_tap.py}: inject faults
in several slots/\allowbreak{}memories and read the whole status word
through the TAP. Bits above the instantiated slot count are a
\textbf{waiver W8} (DFT-only) \\
Serial / peripheral conditions & \code{uart_controller} cond
203/\allowbreak{}238/\allowbreak{}459/\allowbreak{}516 (read of the
write-only TX-data address, \code{tx_pop} while empty, the
\texttt{sb\ \&\ sc} majority pair, framing flag already set),
\code{spi_controller} 195/227 (same two as UART), \code{i2c_controller}
506-512 (push and pop in one cycle), \code{trng}
268/\allowbreak{}310/\allowbreak{}324/\allowbreak{}325 (run-length
reaching the cutoff without a valid sample, flush with word valid, push
and pop together), \code{pwm_controller} 300 (channel enabled with
\texttt{period\ =\allowbreak{}=\allowbreak{}\ 0}), \code{watchdog_timer}
260/269 (read of the feed address; feed while not running) &
simultaneous push/pop and invalid-feed corners & \textbf{Test}, one
directed case each in the owning \code{test_*.py}. Lower risk: all are
reachable and peripheral-local \\
Handshake-stall corners & \code{axi4_to_axilite} conds 172-288,
\code{boot_rom} 150-214, \code{gpu_compute_unit} 242-423, \code{gpu_top}
133-387 (\texttt{valid\ \&\&\ !ready} rows) & a channel stalled by its
partner for several cycles & \textbf{Test}: run the existing suites with
\code{axil_stall_bfm.py} randomised back-pressure (it exists,
\src{tb/cocotb/soc/axil_stall_bfm.py}) on these ports \\
\code{soc_top} instance-level & \code{soc_top.sv} toggle 57.3 \% (167
never-toggled rows): AXI-Lite DMA/GPU address and data bits,
\code{bus_mem_*} high address bits & wide buses, see section 4 & no
action beyond H3 \\
\end{longtable}
}

}

\subsection{Toggle coverage: how it was treated}\label{tm:flow:commercial-sim-coverage:4-toggle-coverage-how-it-was-treated}\origsec{4}

Toggle is the lowest metric (module-definition total 77.0 \%, instance
view 55.0 \%) and the one most dominated by things that cannot or should
not be chased:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Wide data and address buses.} VCS reports a bus as untoggled
  by bit range (\code{prdata[31:10]}, \code{awaddr[28:15]},
  \code{q_mem[2].src[31:0]}); a random-data test cannot reasonably
  toggle every bit of a 32-bit address, and the SoC memory map leaves
  the top address bits constant. These rows are \textbf{counted in the
  scores but not listed as holes}.
\item
  \textbf{Tied-off and constant ports.} \code{pready} (tied 1) and
  \code{pslverr} (tied 0) never toggle on any APB peripheral (30 rows);
  constant AXI sideband (burst, size, len, id, prot, wstrb) 277 rows;
  \code{_unused*} signals. Waiver proposals W3/W4 below. They are tool
  artefacts of zero-wait-state, INCR-only, full-strobe design choices,
  not test gaps.
\item
  \textbf{DFT-only ports.} \code{mbist_status_i}, scan and test-mode
  ports are exercised only in the DFT suites; where a bit exists above
  the instantiated slot count it is waivable (W8). Where it is a real
  status field that can be set, it is a hole (section 3.2, MBIST / DFT
  observability).
\item
  \textbf{What \emph{was} chased.} Toggle rows on \textbf{narrow control
  signals} (response codes, \code{*_stuck}, \code{pll_enable},
  state-encoding bits, queue slots, \code{grp_sel_k}, warp-indexed stack
  slots). Those are H3, H9, H12, H13, H15 above. By classification of
  the 1 860 untoggled rows in the 84 \src{rtl/} modules: 386 are
  wide-bus (\textgreater= 8 bits) ranges, 277 AXI
  sideband/\allowbreak{}unused, 30 tied \code{pready}/\code{pslverr},
  and 1 167 are narrower ranges, of which the large majority are address
  low/high bits, debug-CSR mirror bits (\code{dbg_csr_*},
  \code{mstatus}/\code{mcause} bits the tests never set) and
  \code{pc[1:0]} (always 0 on a 4-byte-aligned ISA). I did not walk all
  1 167; the ones named in section 3 are the control-flow relevant ones.
\item
  The Verilator flow used
  \texttt{-\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}coverage-\allowbreak{}max-\allowbreak{}width\ 256}
  to keep wide arrays out. VCS has no equivalent switch; arrays and
  memories are not toggle-instrumented by default
  (\texttt{-\allowbreak{}cm\_\allowbreak{}tgl\ mda} is off), so
  multi-dimensional memories such as \code{sram_controller.mem} do not
  inflate the denominator.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Proposed waivers (nothing is written; for a human to
accept)}\label{tm:flow:commercial-sim-coverage:5-proposed-waivers-nothing-is-written-for-a-human-to-accept}\origsec{5}

Format follows \src{tools/verif/coverage_waivers.txt} (module, kind,
anchor, category b, justification). VCS does not read that file; the VCS
equivalent is an \texttt{urg\ -\allowbreak{}elfile} exclusion file or
\code{-cm_hier} begin/end blocks, to be generated from the accepted list
so the two flows cannot drift.

{\footnotesize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*49/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*466/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*466/1000\relax}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
ID
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Scope
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Justification
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
W1 & FSM \code{default:} recovery arms (list in 3.2), and the
VCS-inferred arcs into them & the state register only holds named
encodings in simulation (reset value plus enumerated transitions);
reachable only through state corruption. 33 of the 38 \code{default:}
line holes are already waived on the Verilator side (the 5 that are not:
\code{gpu_compute_unit} 499, \code{memory_coalescer} 207,
\code{rv32i_cache_arbiter} 138, \code{rv32i_dcache} 971,
\code{rv32i_decode} 483) \\
W2 & \texttt{\$fatal} generate guards: \code{apb4_register_bank} 61-62,
\code{axi_lite_register_bank} 80-81, \code{axil_to_apb} 93-94,
\code{soc_top} 408 & elaboration-time parameter checks; a passing build
cannot execute them \\
W3 & \code{pready}, \code{pslverr} of APB peripherals & zero-wait-state
peripherals, bus error generated only by the interconnect (H3 covers
that) \\
W4 & constant AXI sideband bits and \code{_unused*} signals & INCR-only,
single-size, full-strobe masters by design \\
W5 & \code{pmu} GPU-domain handshake arms, invalid-state output decode,
counter-while-idle row & the GPU domain has no isolation handshake
(CLAUDE.md item 11, bead \bead{3ogd}: "The GPU needs none"); an invalid
\code{dom_state_d} cannot occur \\
W6 & fully enumerated case defaults: \code{rv32i_decode} 483,
\code{rv32i_imm_gen} 87, \code{rv32i_csr_file}
251/\allowbreak{}259/\allowbreak{}286/\allowbreak{}296/\allowbreak{}597/\allowbreak{}609,
\code{rv32i_pipeline_ex1c} 149/162, \code{rv32i_pipeline_mem} 73/80 &
selector widths or decoder contract make the value unreachable (several
already \code{coverage_off} in RTL) \\
W7 & dead code: \code{dma_engine} 394, \code{rv32i_icache} 510,
\code{rv32i_pipeline_ex1b} 77-83; W7b \code{warp_scheduler} slots
{[}0{]}{[}1..3{]} & provably unreachable (reasoning in section 3).
\textbf{Prefer deleting the dead RTL} over waiving it \\
W8 & DFT-only observability above the instantiated slot count
(\code{mbist_status_i} high bits), scan-mode ports in
\code{dft_ctrl_ports} & exist for a larger configuration; no functional
path in the SoC build \\
W9 & \code{irq_src_i[2]} (timer) at SoC level & tied 0 by design \\
\end{longtable}
}

}

None of these should be applied to raise a number: the bar for each is
"unreachable by construction", and H3-H10 are not waivable (a test can
reach them).

\subsection{Caveats and tool behaviour that affects reading the
numbers}\label{tm:flow:commercial-sim-coverage:6-caveats-and-tool-behaviour-that-affects-reading-the-numbers}\origsec{6}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{C1. Shapes.} 17 modules have more than one parameter shape
  (for example \code{sram_controller} 3, \code{mbist_ctrl} 5,
  \code{apb_interconnect} 5, \code{crypto_accel} 5, \code{mem_collar} 8,
  \code{cdc_gray_fifo} 8). The module score is urg\textquotesingle s
  merged top row; the hole counts are "uncovered in every shape that
  lists the point", so a point hit in only one shape is not listed.
  \code{vcs_cov_holes.py} also records the shape-only holes
  (\code{*_shape_only} keys in its JSON); they are mostly rarely-used
  configurations (for example \code{crypto_accel} with
  \code{EN_AES}/\code{EN_SHA} off).
\item
  \textbf{C2. Generate loops count per instance.} A line inside a
  \texttt{for\ .\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}\ generate} is
  listed once per iteration and counted uncovered if any iteration
  missed it. This is why \code{sram_controller} (128 groups) and
  \code{pmu} (two domains) show line holes that Verilator, which folds
  identical points, did not.
\item
  \textbf{C3. Crypto condition rows.} \code{crypto_accel} L511
  (\code{op_legal_w}) lists row \texttt{1\ 0} ("AES-legal and not
  SHA-legal") as never covered. That contradicts \code{aes128_core} line
  100 \% and 51 passing crypto tests, so it is treated as a
  merge/\allowbreak{}shape bookkeeping artefact of the
  \code{EN_AES}/\code{EN_SHA} parameters and \textbf{not} reported as a
  hole. Needs a per-shape look in Verdi before anything is concluded
  from it.
\item
  \textbf{C4. Missing metric objects.} \code{axi4_crossbar} has no COND
  or BRANCH objects in the vdb (urg: "coverage shape is not there").
  Measured with and without \code{-cm_hier}, so it is not the scope
  file, and a minimal \code{always_comb} \code{if/else} does produce
  both metrics, so it is not a blanket \code{always_comb} limitation.
  The cause was not established (the crossbar uses
  \texttt{for\ (int\ .\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{})} loops
  inside \code{always_comb} and a function call in the condition). Its
  branch and condition coverage is therefore \textbf{not measured} by
  this run; only line, toggle and FSM are.
\item
  \textbf{C5. Verilator pragmas and waivers are not honoured by VCS}
  (see 2.1), so VCS lists defaults that the Verilator report already
  hides.
\item
  \textbf{C6. \texttt{-\allowbreak{}flex\_\allowbreak{}merge\ modules}
  is LCA} and the merged \code{.vdb} is written next to the report
  directory (\code{report/<area>.vdb}). Instance-level hierarchies of
  different testbenches are reported side by side, not merged.
\item
  \textbf{C7. Tests that share a build.} Per-test attribution is
  available (\code{tests.txt}, one row per \code{-cm_name}), but this
  review ranks by module, not by which test hits what.
\item
  \textbf{C8. Not run here:} Xcelium/\allowbreak{}imc coverage (user
  decision); the cocotb functional-coverage YAML export
  (\code{FUNC_COV_DIR} was not set, so no functional percentage is
  quoted for this run); formal; gate-level.
\end{itemize}

\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Item
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Scope

\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead

\endlastfoot
\src{tb/cocotb/soc/func_cov.py} & plumbing over \code{cocotb-coverage}
1.2.0 (coverpoints, crosses, YAML export through \code{FUNC_COV_DIR});
never a hard dependency of a simulation & n/a & opt-in, so an ordinary
run (including this one) exports nothing \\
\src{tb/cocotb/soc/i2c_cov.py} & \textbf{I2C only}: 138 bins sampled
from the BFM event stream, APB transfers and assertion-backed scenario
samples (\src{docs/verification/I2C_FUNCTIONAL_COVERAGE_MODEL.md} (\cref{tm:flow:i2c-functional-coverage-model})) &
\src{tools/verif/functional_coverage_gates.txt}: \code{i2c}, 100 \%,
\code{min_bins} 138 & \code{i2c_controller} is 100 \% line, 95.3 \%
cond, 95.7 \% branch but \textbf{62.5 \% FSM}: the missing arcs (H14)
are the loopback slave\textquotesingle s repeated START, which the model
does not have a bin for (\code{loopback_op} is only
write/\allowbreak{}read). A 100 \% functional gate on a thin model did
not catch it \\
Everything else (UART, SPI, DMA, PMU, crypto, NPU, caches, GPU, fabric)
& no covergroups & none & the holes H1-H10 are all in blocks with
\textbf{no} functional model; code coverage is the only coverage
evidence for them. \src{docs/verification/FUNCTIONAL_COVERAGE.md} (\cref{tm:flow:functional-coverage})
section 4 is the rollout recipe; the highest-value next models are the
AXI/APB fabric (response codes x channel x reset-phase, which would have
flagged H1 and H3), the DMA (queue depth x burst length) and the PMU
(sequence state x reset phase) \\
\end{longtable}
}

}

Code coverage says which code ran; it cannot say that NPU KLEN = 1 and
KLEN = 63 both ran, that all four AES key sizes or all SHA block counts
ran, or that every response code was seen on every channel. Those need
covergroups, which this pass deliberately does not write.

\subsection{Reproducibility and provenance}\label{tm:flow:commercial-sim-coverage:8-reproducibility-and-provenance}\origsec{8}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Instrumented regression and merge: \src{tools/eda/vcs_coverage.py}
  (new), job lists from \src{tools/eda/regress_specs.py}, driver
  \src{tools/eda/run_cocotb_targets.py}, flags in
  \src{tb/cocotb/common/sim_portability.mk}.
\item
  Hole digest: \src{tools/eda/vcs_cov_holes.py} (new) reads only
  urg\textquotesingle s text output.
\item
  Databases and reports are not committed (\src{.gitignore}:
  \code{sim_build*}, \code{*.vdb/}, \code{urgReport*/},
  \code{simv.daidir/}, \code{vcs_cov_scope_*}). They are regenerated by
  the first command in section 1 in about 24 minutes.
\item
  Artifact provenance: the coverage scope lists and \code{-cm_hier}
  files are regenerated by \code{make} at each build from the checked-in
  \src{rtl/} tree; no pre-generated artifact is consumed.
\end{itemize}
\end{relocated}

\begin{relocated}{docs/verification/FUNCTIONAL_COVERAGE.md}
% Converted from docs/verification/FUNCTIONAL_COVERAGE.md by tools/docs/md2tex.py; edit this file, not the original.
\section{Functional coverage in the cocotb flow (bead h367)}\label{tm:flow:functional-coverage}

\src{tools/verif/coverage_report.py} measures Verilator \textbf{code}
coverage (line, toggle). Until this change nothing measured
\textbf{functional} coverage: no covergroup, so the verification
sign-off criterion
\texttt{functional\_\allowbreak{}coverage\_\allowbreak{}pct:\allowbreak{}\ 100}
could not be met for any peripheral. This document records the mechanism
evaluation, the chosen design and how another peripheral adds a model.
The pilot is I2C (\code{I2C_FUNCTIONAL_COVERAGE_MODEL.md} (\cref{tm:flow:i2c-functional-coverage-model})).

\subsection{Evaluation}\label{tm:flow:functional-coverage:1-evaluation}\origsec{1}

{\footnotesize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*348/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*130/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*502/1000\relax}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Option
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Verdict
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Evidence
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\textbf{\code{cocotb-coverage} 1.2.0} (PyPI) & \textbf{Chosen} &
\texttt{Requires-\allowbreak{}Dist:\allowbreak{}\ cocotb\ \textasciitilde{}=\allowbreak{}1.\allowbreak{}8}
(so \code{>=1.8,<2.0}), matching \src{requirements.txt}
(\code{cocotb>=1.8.0,<2.0}) and the installed cocotb 1.9.2. Pure Python;
\code{cocotb_coverage.coverage} does not even import cocotb. CoverPoint
/ CoverCross / \code{at_least} / weights, YAML and XML export, and a
\code{merge_coverage} for runs. Verified here: installs, imports,
samples inside a Verilator cocotb 1.9.2 simulation, exports, and its
merge agrees with this repo\textquotesingle s merge (a unit test). \\
\code{cocotb-coverage} 2.0 (2025-10-03) & Not usable now & Requires
\code{cocotb>=2} and Python \code{>=3.11}. This repo is pinned to cocotb
1.x (cocotb 2 removed the \code{units=} kwarg used throughout \src{tb/})
and CI runs Python 3.10. Revisit when the cocotb 2 migration happens;
the YAML schema is unchanged, so only \code{func_cov.py} would be
touched. \\
pyuvm (4.0.1 installed, \code{<3.1} pinned) & No & No covergroup
facility: the package has the UVM component, phase, TLM, factory and
register-model classes only (a search for covergroup / coverpoint over
the installed package finds nothing). \\
SystemVerilog covergroups under Verilator & No & Verilator 5.048 (the
flake pin) parses and \textbf{ignores} them:
\texttt{\%Warning-\allowbreak{}COVERIGN:\allowbreak{}\ Ignoring\ unsupported:\allowbreak{}\ coverpoint}
/ \texttt{cover\ bin\ specification} /
\texttt{coverage\ clocking\ event} (reproduced with a 2-bin covergroup).
They would also have to live in RTL or an SV testbench, which this pilot
must not touch. \\
In-repo collector & No & Would re-implement bins, crosses,
\code{at_least}, merge and export that the library already has and
tests. The one design hedge kept: the merge/\allowbreak{}gate script
reads the plain exported YAML schema and does \textbf{not} import the
library, so the sampling layer can be replaced without touching the
gate. \\
\end{longtable}
}

}

\textbf{Recommendation: \code{cocotb-coverage==1.2.0}}, pinned exactly
in \src{requirements.txt} (1.x is frozen at 1.2.0, 2023-11-15; 2.0 is
the maintained line but needs cocotb 2).

Known library traits that shaped the glue
(\src{tb/cocotb/soc/func_cov.py}):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \code{CoverageDB} is a process-wide singleton and cocotb runs every
  test of a MODULE in one simulator process, so the database is
  cumulative across a suite; export once, at process exit.
\item
  Sampling is by decorated function. \code{CoverPoint} runs
  \code{inspect.getmembers} on the first argument the first time it is
  called, so samplers take plain Python values (never a DUT handle).
\item
  The first matching bin wins (\code{inj}), which makes ordered range
  bins exclusive.
\item
  A cross reads the \code{new_hits} of its coverpoints, so the
  coverpoints must be sampled first (\code{func_cov.pair} orders the
  decorators correctly).
\item
  Cross bins are exported as \code{str(tuple)} keys, for example
  \texttt{('write',\allowbreak{}\ 'ack')}.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Flow}\label{tm:flow:functional-coverage:2-flow}\origsec{2}

\begin{lstlisting}[language={}]
 cocotb test --> BFM observers --> func_cov samplers --> coverage_db (per process)
                                                              | atexit, if FUNC_COV_DIR is set
                                                              v
                                       $FUNC_COV_DIR/<module>__<pid>.yml   (one per simulator process)
                                                              | CI: uploaded by each shard
                                                              v
        tools/verif/functional_coverage_report.py  --gate  -->  report .md/.json + merged .yml
\end{lstlisting}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Sampling is on by default and costs a few dict updates per event.
  \textbf{Export is opt-in} through \code{FUNC_COV_DIR}; unset means
  nothing is written, so a local run leaves no files.
\item
  If \code{cocotb_coverage} is not installed every sampler is a no-op
  and the suites still run. The CI gate is what fails (a required group
  with no data is exit 2), so a missing package cannot turn it green.
\item
  Local run:
  \texttt{FUNC\_\allowbreak{}COV\_\allowbreak{}DIR=\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}tmp/\allowbreak{}fc\ nix\ develop\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}command\ make\ -\allowbreak{}C\ tb/\allowbreak{}cocotb/\allowbreak{}soc\ i2c},
  then
  \texttt{python3\ tools/\allowbreak{}verif/\allowbreak{}functional\_\allowbreak{}coverage\_\allowbreak{}report.\allowbreak{}py\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}in\ /\allowbreak{}tmp/\allowbreak{}fc\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}waivers\ tools/\allowbreak{}verif/\allowbreak{}functional\_\allowbreak{}coverage\_\allowbreak{}waivers.\allowbreak{}txt\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}gates\ tools/\allowbreak{}verif/\allowbreak{}functional\_\allowbreak{}coverage\_\allowbreak{}gates.\allowbreak{}txt\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}gate}.
  \code{PYTHONPATH} must reach a Python that has \code{cocotb-coverage}
  (it is in \src{requirements.txt}).
\item
  CI: \code{cocotb.yml} (per pull request). Each \code{soc_shard} leg
  sets \code{FUNC_COV_DIR}, installs the package and uploads
  \code{func_cov/}; the \code{soc_all} aggregate job runs the gate over
  all shards and publishes the report as the \code{functional-coverage}
  artifact (and in the job summary). The instrumented
  \code{soc_coverage.yml} is not used: sampling needs no instrumented
  build, and cocotb.yml already runs per pull request.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{The gate}\label{tm:flow:functional-coverage:3-the-gate}\origsec{3}

\texttt{functional\_\allowbreak{}coverage\_\allowbreak{}report.\allowbreak{}py\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}gate}
merges every YAML (hits add; totals are recomputed from the hits, never
taken from the inputs; a coverpoint declared differently in two files is
exit 2), then:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \src{tools/verif/functional_coverage_gates.txt}
  (\texttt{group\ |\ floor\_\allowbreak{}pct\ |\ min\_\allowbreak{}bins\ |\ why}):
  every listed group is \textbf{required} (no data is exit 2), its
  adjusted percentage must reach \code{floor_pct}, and its model must
  keep at least \code{min_bins} bins, so deleting coverpoints cannot buy
  a pass.
\item
  \src{tools/verif/functional_coverage_waivers.txt}
  (\texttt{item\ |\ bin\ |\ b\ |\ why}): category \code{b} only,
  unreachable by design, with a reason. A bin that is merely not hit
  needs a test. A waiver for a bin that does not exist, or that was hit,
  is stale and fails the gate.
\end{itemize}

Exit status: 0 pass, 1 gate red, 2 could not evaluate (same convention
as \code{coverage_report.py}).

\subsection{Rolling out to another peripheral}\label{tm:flow:functional-coverage:4-rolling-out-to-another-peripheral}\origsec{4}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Model first.} Write
  \code{docs/verification/<PERIPH>_FUNCTIONAL_COVERAGE_MODEL.md}: one
  row per coverpoint with its bins and why each matters (spec section or
  RTL header). Derive bins from the register map and the behaviours the
  spec promises, not from what the tests happen to do.
\item
  \textbf{\code{tb/cocotb/soc/<periph>_cov.py}}, modelled on
  \code{i2c_cov.py}: declare the coverpoints at module level with
  \code{func_cov.point} / \code{ranges} / \code{pair} (names
  \code{<periph>.<group>.<coverpoint>}), put the samplers in a small
  observer class, create
  \texttt{COV\ =\allowbreak{}\ .\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}}
  and call
  \texttt{func\_\allowbreak{}cov.\allowbreak{}enable\_\allowbreak{}export(os.\allowbreak{}environ.\allowbreak{}get("MODULE",\allowbreak{}\ "<periph>"))}.
\item
  \textbf{Sampling points, in order of preference:} a. observers on the
  existing BFMs. \code{APB4Master.add_observer(cb)} gives
  \texttt{cb(is\_\allowbreak{}write,\allowbreak{}\ addr,\allowbreak{}\ data,\allowbreak{}\ strb,\allowbreak{}\ ok)}
  after every transfer; a pin-level BFM can offer the same
  (\code{I2CSlave.add_observer}). Sample only fields the byte strobes
  cover. b. the tests\textquotesingle{} side-effect-free register peek,
  via
  \texttt{COV.\allowbreak{}reg\_\allowbreak{}read(addr,\allowbreak{}\ value)}.
  c. an explicit scenario call
  (\texttt{COV.\allowbreak{}arb\_\allowbreak{}loss("data\_\allowbreak{}bit")})
  \textbf{only} for a fact the pins cannot show, placed \textbf{after}
  the test\textquotesingle s own assertions for that scenario, so a
  failing scenario samples nothing.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{COV.\allowbreak{}attach(apb,\allowbreak{}\ bfm)}}
  where the test builds its BFMs (per test: a new BFM per test, the
  database is per process).
\item
  \textbf{Run it, read the holes.} Each uncovered bin is a test to write
  or, if it cannot happen, a category-\code{b} waiver with the reason.
  Mutation-check any new test as the other suites are.
\item
  Add the group to \code{functional_coverage_gates.txt} with the
  measured \code{min_bins} (and a floor of 100 after waivers), and the
  waivers to \code{functional_coverage_waivers.txt}. If you add a cocotb
  suite, add it to \code{SOC_CI_SUITES} in \src{tb/cocotb/soc/Makefile}
  and to \src{tools/verif/soc_shard_weights.json} and
  \code{soc_shard_weights_plain.json}.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{What this does not do}\label{tm:flow:functional-coverage:5-what-this-does-not-do}\origsec{5}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Coverage is only as good as the model: 100 \% of a thin model is not
  verification closure. Bins come from the spec; the model document says
  what each one is for.
\item
  Scenario samples (3c) are declarations backed by the
  test\textquotesingle s assertions, not observations.
\item
  No cross-run history or trend; the report is per run (the merged YAML
  is the artifact).
\item
  Only I2C is modelled. Other peripherals have no covergroups until they
  follow section 4.
\end{itemize}
\end{relocated}

\begin{relocated}{docs/verification/I2C_FUNCTIONAL_COVERAGE_MODEL.md}
% Converted from docs/verification/I2C_FUNCTIONAL_COVERAGE_MODEL.md by tools/docs/md2tex.py; edit this file, not the original.
\section{I2C functional-coverage model (bead h367 pilot)}\label{tm:flow:i2c-functional-coverage-model}

DUT: \src{rtl/periph/i2c_controller.sv} (+ \code{i2c_bit_engine.sv}),
I2C master, APB4 slave, 12 registers. Implementation:
\src{tb/cocotb/soc/i2c_cov.py}. Mechanism, flow and rollout:
\code{FUNCTIONAL_COVERAGE.md} (\cref{tm:flow:functional-coverage}). The bins come from the controller header
(the spec of record for this IP) and the I2C protocol, not from what the
tests happen to do. Names are \code{i2c.<group>.<coverpoint>}.

Sources of samples: \textbf{bus} = the \code{I2CSlave} BFM event stream;
\textbf{apb} = \code{APB4Master} transfers and the
tests\textquotesingle{} \code{_peek}; \textbf{scenario} = a call placed
after the test\textquotesingle s own assertions. No RTL is touched and
no DUT signal is read by the model.

Result of the first complete run: \textbf{138 bins, all hit, no waivers}
(table at the end).

\subsection{bus: what the master did on the wire (source: bus)}\label{tm:flow:i2c-functional-coverage-model:bus-what-the-master-did-on-the-wire-source-bus}

{\footnotesize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*272/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*342/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*366/1000\relax}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Coverpoint
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Bins
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Why
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\code{start_kind} & \code{start}, \code{rstart_after_write},
\code{rstart_after_read} & START and the two repeated-START contexts. A
START that follows an aborted transfer is not counted as a repeated
START. \\
\code{addr_dir}, \code{addr_ack}, cross \code{addr_result} &
write/\allowbreak{}read x ack/nack (4 cross bins) & Address phase
outcome per direction. \\
\code{wr_data_ack} & ack, nack & A slave NACK on write data. \\
\code{rd_master_ack} & ack, nack & The master ACKs every read byte
except a NACK\_LAST final one. \\
\code{wr_bytes} & 0 (address only), 1, 2, 3-4, 5-8, 9-16, 17+ & Per
write segment with an ACKed address; 8 is the FIFO depth, 9-16 and 17+
need FIFO refill during the transfer. Counts every data byte on the
wire. \\
\code{rd_bytes} & 1, 2, 3-4, 5-8, 9-16, 17+ & Same for reads (RX FIFO
full / drain). \\
\code{segments_per_txn} & 1, 2, 3+ & START..STOP: plain, one repeated
START, a chain. \\
\code{stop_after} & write, read, addr\_nack & What the STOP closed. \\
\code{stretch_at} & addr\_bit, addr\_ack, wr\_bit, wr\_ack, rd\_bit,
rd\_ack & Mandatory clock stretching, where the slave held SCL.
\code{STRETCH} is reported by the BFM (not in \code{events}). \\
\code{stretch_mode} & single\_\allowbreak{}fall,
every\_\allowbreak{}fall & One stretch vs a slave that stretches every
bit. \\
\end{longtable}
}

}

\subsection{Known limits of the model}\label{tm:flow:i2c-functional-coverage-model:known-limits-of-the-model}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  It checks that scenarios occurred, not that the DUT\textquotesingle s
  data was right: that stays the tests\textquotesingle{} job.
\item
  \code{arb_loss}, \code{timeout_cause}, \code{cmd_ignored},
  \code{abort} and \code{soc.path} are declarations made after the
  test\textquotesingle s assertions, not independent observations.
\item
  Not modelled: back-to-back START timing (tBUF/\allowbreak{}tHD;STA,
  measured by dedicated tests), 10-bit addressing (not implemented),
  multi-master beyond arbitration loss, SDA setup/\allowbreak{}hold.
\end{itemize}
\end{relocated}

\begin{relocated}{docs/verification/SOC_COVERAGE_REPORT.md}
% Converted from docs/verification/SOC_COVERAGE_REPORT.md by tools/docs/md2tex.py; edit this file, not the original.
\section{SoC line + toggle coverage report (GH \#216)}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report}

Bead \code{nkj7}. Measured 2026-10-06 on branch
\code{feat/soc-coverage-216}.
\texttt{Doc:\allowbreak{}\ ds:\allowbreak{}ch:\allowbreak{}verification-\allowbreak{}plan\ (line\ 362\ of\ the\ original\ file)}
sets a

\begin{quote}
95 \% RTL line-coverage exit criterion; until now only the 9 CPU cocotb
modules (\texttt{sim/\allowbreak{}Makefile\ coverage}) were
instrumented. This report covers the whole \src{tb/cocotb/soc}
regression (\code{soc_all_ci}).
\end{quote}

\subsection{Method}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:method}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \code{COVERAGE=1} in \src{tb/cocotb/soc/Makefile} adds
  \texttt{-\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}coverage-\allowbreak{}line\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}coverage-\allowbreak{}toggle\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}coverage-\allowbreak{}max-\allowbreak{}width\ 256}
  to \code{COMPILE_ARGS} (not \code{EXTRA_ARGS}: most targets override
  that on the command line). Every \code{SIM_BUILD} (since bead
  \bead{b079} a content-keyed \code{sim_build_<TOPLEVEL>_<hash>}, shared
  by suites with an identical model; the \code{.dat} name carries MODULE
  so sharing does not clash) gets a \code{_cov} suffix, so an
  instrumented build can never reuse an uninstrumented \code{Vtop} (the
  \prref{142} stale-\code{Vtop} false-pass class). Each simulation writes
  its own \code{<build>__<module>.dat} through the runtime plusarg
  \code{+verilator+coverage+file+} (honoured by cocotb
  1.9.2\textquotesingle s \code{verilator.cpp}).
  \texttt{-\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}coverage-\allowbreak{}max-\allowbreak{}width\ 256}
  keeps the DMA\textquotesingle s 8192-bit \code{linebuf} and the SRAM
  model arrays out of toggle coverage.
\item
  \texttt{make\ soc\_\allowbreak{}coverage} runs the whole
  \code{soc_all_ci} instrumented, merges all \code{.dat} files with
  \texttt{verilator\_\allowbreak{}coverage\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}write},
  and renders the tables below with
  \src{tools/verif/coverage_report.py}. It is not part of
  \code{soc_all_ci} / \code{soc_all}; CI runs it nightly
  (\src{.github/workflows/soc_coverage.yml}).
\item
  \textbf{CI is sharded (bead \bead{9ayu.1}).} The workflow does not run
  \code{soc_coverage} as one job: it runs \code{NSHARDS} (8)
  \code{soc_shard} legs
  (\texttt{make\ soc\_\allowbreak{}coverage\_\allowbreak{}shard\ SHARD=\allowbreak{}k\ NSHARDS=\allowbreak{}8})
  and the \code{cpu_gpu} job in parallel, then a final job (name
  \texttt{SoC\ line\ +\allowbreak{}\ toggle\ coverage}, the required
  check) merges every shard\textquotesingle s \code{.dat} with the same
  \texttt{verilator\_\allowbreak{}coverage\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}write}
  and runs the unchanged report and gate. The suite list is the single
  variable \code{SOC_CI_SUITES} in \src{tb/cocotb/soc/Makefile}
  (\code{soc_all_ci} runs all of it). The slice for shard k comes from
  \src{tools/verif/soc_shards.py} (deterministic greedy balance on the
  measured timings in \src{tools/verif/soc_shard_weights.json}; a new
  suite needs no edit there, it gets a default weight). The final job
  fails unless the union of the suites the shards ran equals
  \code{SOC_CI_SUITES}
  (\texttt{soc\_\allowbreak{}shards.\allowbreak{}py\ union}) and the
  pass count SUMMED over all shard logs reaches \code{PASS_FLOOR}.
  \texttt{make\ soc\_\allowbreak{}coverage} itself is unchanged for
  local use.
\item
  Line \% = \code{v_line} + \code{v_branch} points; toggle \% =
  \code{v_toggle} points (per bit, per direction, as Verilator counts
  them). A point is the same point in every instance and testbench and
  is hit if any instance hit it; \code{<module>__<params>}
  specialisations fold into the base module.
\item
  Triaged trees: \src{rtl/soc}, \src{rtl/periph}, \src{rtl/npu} (line +
  control-toggle gate). \src{rtl/cpu}, \src{rtl/mem}, \src{rtl/gpu}:
  \textbf{line gate only} since bead \bead{a5ze} slice 2 (see "Gate
  decision"); no toggle gate, no toggle triage. Excluded: \src{tb/},
  \src{sim/}, behavioural SRAM models.
\end{itemize}

Exact run command (15 GB host, \code{SIM_BUILD_ROOT} on the big disk,
never concurrent with another soc sim):

\begin{lstlisting}[language=bash]
nix develop --command make -C tb/cocotb/soc soc_coverage SIM_BUILD_ROOT=/nobackup/claude_sim_build/soc_cov
# outputs: $SIM_BUILD_ROOT/coverage/{merged.dat,coverage_report.md,coverage_report.json}
\end{lstlisting}

Measured run: 53 suites, \textbf{480 passed, 0 failed, 16 skipped} (the
same 480/0/16 as the uninstrumented regression), 53 per-simulation
\code{.dat} files merged, wall time about 85 min (5 095 s) with a cold
build, 5.9 GB of build output. Nix Verilator 5.048 handled
\code{--coverage} without the bead \bead{hg2} crash: the Makefile
already passes
\texttt{PYTHON3=\allowbreak{}\$(SIM\_\allowbreak{}PYTHON3)}, and
\code{verilator_coverage} from the same nix profile merges fine, so no
source-built Verilator is needed.

\textbf{Re-measured 2026-10-07 after bead \bead{8riq}} (same command,
\code{SIM_BUILD_ROOT} from clean, all other RTL unchanged): 54 suites (+
the new \code{axil_apb_fabric}), \textbf{511 passed by the CI log-grep
formula, 0 failed, 17 skipped} (512 \code{PASS} rows in the
\code{TESTS=} blocks: the extra one is the \code{expect_fail} guard
\code{test_ring_unmapped_read_single_beat}, which cocotb reports as a
pass but the CI grep does not count; +31 = 14 fabric + 6
register\_\allowbreak{}bank + 2 axil\_\allowbreak{}to\_\allowbreak{}apb
+ 10 apb\_\allowbreak{}interconnect sweep points, minus that 1,
reconciling exactly against the prior 480). 55 \code{.dat} files merged.

The formerly uncovered points now hit: \code{axi_lite_interconnect} L206
(W\_AW stall), L207 (W\_DATA stall), L326 (R\_AR stall);
\code{axi_lite_register_bank} L156/L158 (\code{w_now} low: W captured
before AW) and L205 (\code{RD_RESP} with \code{rready} low);
\code{axil_to_apb} L175/\allowbreak{}L176/\allowbreak{}L177 (ACCESS wait
state, \code{S_WRESP}/\code{S_RRESP} held by a stalled master).
\code{s_axil_bresp}/\code{rresp} of the ring now leave OKAY end to end
(EXOKAY/\allowbreak{}SLVERR/\allowbreak{}DECERR from a slave; DECERR
from the ring\textquotesingle s own decode; SLVERR from
\code{axil_to_apb} for a slave \code{pslverr} and for
\code{apb_interconnect}\textquotesingle s unclaimed-slot
\code{pslverr_o}). \code{apb_interconnect}
\code{pwdata_o}/\code{pstrb_o}/\code{pwrite_o}/\code{penable_o}/\code{paddr_o}
are now driven and checked on every slave copy. The two
\code{axi_lite_register_bank} response ports are waived (tied to OKAY by
design, one new category-b waiver); the rest of the toggle gain is real
stimulus.

\textbf{Waiver correction (partially addresses \ghref{222}, W1).} The
\code{soc_bus} waiver
\texttt{\textasciicircum{}(apb\_\allowbreak{}pslverr|apb\_\allowbreak{}m\_\allowbreak{}pslverr)[\textbackslash{}[:\allowbreak{}]}
was too wide: \code{apb_m_pslverr} is
\code{apb_interconnect}\textquotesingle s \code{pslverr_o}, which is 1
on an unmapped APB access, so it is not a hard-wired constant. It is
narrowed to
\texttt{\textasciicircum{}apb\_\allowbreak{}pslverr[\textbackslash{}[:\allowbreak{}]}
and \code{apb_m_pslverr} (2 points) now shows as a never-toggling signal
in \code{soc_bus}. It still does not toggle in the full regression, and
the reason is the memory map, not a missing test: the only hole in the
APB window (\code{0x2000_5000-0x2000_5FFF}) is claimed by the DMA slot
of the AXI-Lite ring (last match wins in \code{axi_lite_interconnect}),
so no ring access can ever reach an unclaimed APB offset in the
integrated SoC, and every APB slave ties \code{pslverr} to 0. The same
\code{apb_interconnect} -\textgreater{} \code{axil_to_apb}
-\textgreater{} SLVERR path is covered in \code{axil_apb_fabric}, which
instantiates the real modules with a hole in the APB window. Making
\code{soc_bus.apb_m_pslverr} toggle would need either a map change or an
unmapped-APB stimulus injected below the ring; \textbf{Decided
2026-10-07 (user): waive, do not change the map} -\/-
\code{apb_m_pslverr} is now waived in \code{soc_bus} with this map-based
justification (W1 closed). Validated against \prref{226}\textquotesingle s
CI coverage data: the waiver matches, no waiver is stale, and
\code{apb_m_pslverr} no longer appears as uncovered.

Non-vacuity: the known gaps show up in the raw (pre-waiver) report:
\code{timer_irq} never toggles, \code{i2c_scl_o} / \code{i2c_sda_o}
never toggle, and the DMA early-RLAST branch is not in the uncovered
list (covered since bead \bead{wdmo}, \prref{217}).
