Formerly uncovered \code{soc_top} points now hit (all behaviourally
checked, not just toggled):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \code{soc_top.sv} L1450-L1456 (GPU isolation clamps, 7
  \code{cond_then} arms): PMU GPU power cycle with
  \texttt{gpu\_\allowbreak{}irq\_\allowbreak{}o\ =\allowbreak{}=\allowbreak{}\ gpu\_\allowbreak{}irq\_\allowbreak{}raw\ \&\ !gpu\_\allowbreak{}iso\_\allowbreak{}en}
  asserted on every cycle and the clamp seen acting
  (\texttt{raw=\allowbreak{}1,\allowbreak{}\ out=\allowbreak{}0,\allowbreak{}\ iso=\allowbreak{}1})
  in the iso-before-reset window; sequence order
  \texttt{ret\_\allowbreak{}save\ <\ iso\_\allowbreak{}en\ <\ clk\ gate\ <\ reset}
  down and the reverse up checked from the testbench.
\item
  \code{pmu_gpu_iso_en}, \code{pmu_gpu_ret_save},
  \code{pmu_gpu_ret_restore}, \code{timer_irq} (+\code{_cpu_sync}),
  \code{uart_irq}, \code{spi_irq}, \code{dma_irq}: the CPU takes the
  trap for each (ISR committed, \code{mcause} 0x8000\_\allowbreak{}0007
  for the timer\textquotesingle s direct MTIP and
  0x8000\_\allowbreak{}000B for the three routed through
  \code{interrupt_controller}, pending bit logged, source nets sampled
  at trap entry and after the handler).
\item
  \code{spi_miso_i}: non-loopback SPI against a testbench slave;
  firmware reads back 0xA6, the slave checks MOSI 0x3C and CS framing.
\item
  Debug path
  \texttt{apb\_\allowbreak{}\{psel,\allowbreak{}penable,\allowbreak{}pwrite,\allowbreak{}paddr,\allowbreak{}pwdata\}\_\allowbreak{}i},
  \code{dbg_bridge_m_*}, and \textbf{\code{apb_pslverr_o},
  \code{dbg_bridge_m_pslverr}} (un-waived by \ghref{222} W2): PSLVERR on a
  write to each RO CSR debug register (\code{rv32i_cpu_top.sv:64,330})
  and the \code{apb_cdc_bridge} force-complete PSLVERR with the
  destination (CPU-domain) in reset, both before the request and with
  the reset asserted 1-6 cycles into it.
\item
  \code{pll_m_pwdata}/\code{pll2_m_pwdata}/\code{paddr} and
  \code{pll_subsystem}/\code{pll_apb_regs} divider fields: both
  PLLs\textquotesingle{} CONTROL written through the fabric (read back,
  locked flag re-checked), \code{pll_fb_div}/\code{pll_post_div} of each
  instance checked after every write and the other instance proven
  untouched. \code{pll_clkgen_stub} is a pass-through by design, so the
  "output" observed is the divider ports at \code{pll_clkgen} plus the
  lock flag, not a frequency change.
\end{itemize}

Still open in this bead\textquotesingle s scope: \code{i2c_*_oe_o} (pad
drive, bead \bead{pnfw}), ROM write channel \code{bus_rom_aw*/w*/b*}
(bead \bead{ej6j}, unit level), \code{uart_rx_i} (bead \bead{rqvo}),
\code{pll_m_prdata}/\code{pready} partial points.
\code{interrupt_controller} and \code{pmu} toggle gains are small
because their uncovered points are register-bank
data/\allowbreak{}address bits, not integration paths.

\textbf{Finding (no RTL change made).} The GPU\textquotesingle s flops
are async-reset, so the PMU\textquotesingle s domain reset clears all
GPU state (the level-held irq latch, \code{irq_enable}, \code{done})
even though the clock is gated first. \code{pmu.sv}\textquotesingle s
header and \src{docs/POWER_DOMAIN_EVALUATION.md} (\cref{tm:pd:power-domain-evaluation}) claim state survives an
off/on cycle "by construction" because reset only asserts under a gated
clock; that holds for the synchronous-reset CPU but not for the GPU.
\code{test_soc_gpu_isolation_via_pmu} asserts the actual (state-lost)
behaviour and a successful relaunch; tracked as a
documentation/\allowbreak{}model-fidelity bead (the physical behaviour
of a power-gated domain with no retention cells is state loss anyway).

\subsection{Results}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:results}

\texttt{make\ soc\_\allowbreak{}coverage} on branch
\code{test/register-walk-7ovx} (59 simulation runs, 597 passed / 0
failed / 17 skipped by the CI log-grep formula, 59 \code{.dat} files
merged), rendered with the unchanged
\src{tools/verif/coverage_report.py} and the waiver file after the 7ovx
drift fix (below). The tables are therefore a single consistent
measurement; the earlier per-bead dagger rows (unit-only
\code{dma_engine}, \code{uart_controller}, \code{spi_controller}) are
superseded by it. The PR\textquotesingle s own CI \code{soc_coverage}
run supersedes these numbers.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{Combined report: SoC + CPU / cache / GPU
suites (bead \code{1eyv},
2026-10-10)}{Combined report: SoC + CPU / cache / GPU suites (bead , 2026-10-10)}}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:combined-report-soc-cpu-cache-gpu-suites-bead-1eyv-2026-10-10}

The informational rows above reflect only what the \code{soc_*} suites
incidentally hit. The CPU, Phase 3 cache and Phase 4 GPU suites were
never part of this report (the nightly \code{rtl-coverage} job measured
only the CPU modules, and its \texttt{make\ coverage} kept just the last
simulation\textquotesingle s \code{sim/coverage.dat}; GPU had no
coverage wiring at all). This section merges them in.

\subsubsection{Method}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:method-1}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Same toolchain, same flags.} \src{sim/Makefile} now takes
  \code{COVERAGE=1} exactly like the SoC Makefile:
  \texttt{-\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}coverage-\allowbreak{}line\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}coverage-\allowbreak{}toggle\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}coverage-\allowbreak{}max-\allowbreak{}width\ 256}
  in \code{COMPILE_ARGS}, a \code{_cov} suffix on every \code{SIM_BUILD}
  (idempotent, because cocotb exports \code{SIM_BUILD} and a sub-make
  would otherwise append twice; instrumented and plain \code{Vtop} can
  never be reused for each other), and one \code{<build>__<module>.dat}
  per simulation through \code{+verilator+coverage+file+}. It replaces
  the blanket \code{--coverage} and the
  every-simulation-overwrites-\code{sim/coverage.dat} behaviour.
  \code{SIM_PYTHON3} is now overridable (CI needs it).
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{make\ -\allowbreak{}C\ sim\ coverage\_\allowbreak{}cpu\_\allowbreak{}gpu}}
  runs the 9 CPU modules + Phase 3 cache suites (\texttt{make\ test})
  and \code{gpu_all} (unit, kernels, handoff, random smoke)
  instrumented, and merges them into \code{cpu_gpu_merged.dat}. The
  existing \texttt{make\ coverage} (nightly \code{rtl-coverage},
  \code{sim/coverage_merged.dat} artifact) is kept, now also with
  per-simulation \code{.dat} files instead of losing the Phase 3 ones.
\item
  \textbf{Merge at the report level, not with
  \texttt{verilator\_\allowbreak{}coverage\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}write}.}
  \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}verif/\allowbreak{}coverage\_\allowbreak{}report.\allowbreak{}py\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}dat\ soc/\allowbreak{}merged.\allowbreak{}dat\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}dat\ cpu\_\allowbreak{}gpu\_\allowbreak{}merged.\allowbreak{}dat}
  concatenates the inputs and aggregates them like instances of one run.
  The report already identified a point as (module, file, kind, line,
  column, object, source lines) with the hierarchy dropped and "hit if
  any hit", so a point of \code{rtl/cpu/...} measured by both flows is
  one point, hit if either hit it, with nothing new to reconcile. A raw
  \texttt{verilator\_\allowbreak{}coverage\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}write}
  would instead need identical absolute paths \emph{and} hierarchy keys
  to fold points, and the two flows differ in both: the SoC \code{.dat}
  records \code{tb_soc_top.u_soc.u_cpu...}, the CPU one
  \code{Vtop.rv32i_cpu_top...}, and the files carry whatever checkout
  root the run used (a developer worktree such as
  \code{.claude/worktrees/agent-xxx/}, \code{/home/runner/work/...} on
  CI, \code{../rtl/...} from \src{sim/}).
\item
  \textbf{Path normalisation} (new): a path under \code{--root} is made
  repo-relative; any other path is anchored on its
  \texttt{rtl/\allowbreak{}<soc|periph|npu|cpu|mem|gpu>/\allowbreak{}}
  component. Before, a \code{.dat} from another checkout (including
  every CI artifact opened locally) silently dropped all RTL as "outside
  root".
\item
  \textbf{No vacuous merge} (new): an input contributing zero reportable
  RTL points is an error naming the input; modules measured by more than
  one input have their point sets compared and listed under
  \emph{Cross-input point-set consistency}, flagged \code{SUSPECT} below
  80 \% overlap (a union of two disagreeing sets would double the
  denominator and under-report), and the CLI warns on stderr. The union
  is never shrunk to the overlap and hits are never credited across
  non-matching points.
\item
  \textbf{Versions.} The nightly \code{rtl-coverage} job builds
  Verilator \textbf{5.036} from source; \code{soc_coverage} uses the
  flake-pinned nix \textbf{5.048}. Point keys are not guaranteed to
  match across them (not verifiable here: only 5.048 is installed). So
  the combined CI run does \emph{not} consume the
  \code{rtl-coverage-report} artifact: it runs \code{coverage_cpu_gpu}
  on the same nix Verilator inside \code{soc_coverage.yml}. Measured:
  with both inputs on 5.048, \textbf{every module measured by both flows
  has an identical point set} (consistency section empty), which is the
  check that the merge is exact rather than assumed. Cost: about 14 min
  and 1.5 GB for the CPU/\allowbreak{}cache/\allowbreak{}GPU half (29
  \code{TESTS=} blocks, 207 tests, 0 failed), against 85 min for the SoC
  half.
\end{enumerate}

SoC input: the 2026-10-09 \texttt{bead\ 7ovx} full \code{soc_coverage}
run (597 passed by the CI formula, 0 failed), reused rather than re-run.
\texttt{git\ diff\ 745574e\ HEAD\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}\ rtl} is
empty, so the RTL it measured is exactly this tree\textquotesingle s,
and the tests are the \code{soc_all_ci} set at \code{394db87}. Its
\code{.dat} records another worktree\textquotesingle s absolute paths,
which is itself the path-normalisation case. Triaged-tree numbers from
this combined run are identical to the SoC-only run (the CPU/GPU suites
add nothing to \texttt{rtl/\allowbreak{}soc|periph|npu}): 42 modules,
line 1667/\allowbreak{}1669, toggle 41334/\allowbreak{}59656.

\begin{lstlisting}[language=bash]
nix develop --command make -C tb/cocotb/soc soc_coverage SIM_BUILD_ROOT=/nobackup/claude_sim_build/soc_cov
nix develop --command make -C sim coverage_cpu_gpu SIM_BUILD_ROOT=/nobackup/claude_sim_build/cov_cpu_gpu
python3 tools/verif/coverage_report.py \
    --dat /nobackup/claude_sim_build/soc_cov/coverage/merged.dat \
    --dat /nobackup/claude_sim_build/cov_cpu_gpu/coverage/cpu_gpu_merged.dat \
    --waivers tools/verif/coverage_waivers.txt --out-md combined.md --out-json combined.json
# or in one go:  make -C sim coverage_combined SOC_DAT=<soc merged.dat> SIM_BUILD_ROOT=...
\end{lstlisting}

What the combined numbers say that the SoC-only ones hid: the large
"gaps" in \code{rv32i_decode}, \code{rv32i_alu}, \code{vector_alu},
\code{shared_memory} and the GPU toggle figures were just missing
suites. What they now show as genuine, unwaived gaps (bead \bead{a5ze}
tracks the triage): \code{rv32i_csr_file} (70.5 \% line, 39.0 \%
toggle), \code{rv32i_pipeline_mem} (88.6 \%), \code{rv32i_pipeline_ex1b}
(83.3 \%, unchanged by every suite), \code{gpu_top} (86.9 \%),
\code{vector_alu} (90.5 \%), \code{warp_scheduler} (40.1 \% toggle),
\code{rv32i_interrupt_ctrl} (32.1 \% toggle, no suite moves it) and
\code{gpu_command_queue} (43.9 \% toggle). Not yet triaged: some may be
unreachable by design and need waivers, none are assumed to be.

\subsubsection{CI and recommendation}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:ci-and-recommendation}

\src{.github/workflows/soc_coverage.yml} now runs
\texttt{make\ -\allowbreak{}C\ sim\ coverage\_\allowbreak{}cpu\_\allowbreak{}gpu}
on the same nix Verilator after the SoC run (\code{continue-on-error}),
renders the combined report, appends it to the job summary and uploads
it as the separate artifact \code{rtl-combined-coverage}. The existing
\code{soc-coverage} artifact, per-flow report, \code{PASS_FLOOR} and
gate are untouched, and the nightly \code{rtl-coverage} job in
\code{random_tests.yml} is unchanged. Everything stays non-gating;
\texttt{rtl/\allowbreak{}cpu|mem|gpu} stay \textbf{informational} in the
report (the recorded user decision: triage covers
\texttt{rtl/\allowbreak{}soc|periph|npu} only; this change does not
alter it).

\textbf{Recommendation (not applied): triage \src{rtl/cpu},
\src{rtl/mem} and \src{rtl/gpu} for line coverage, still not gating.}
The reason the user\textquotesingle s decision was sound, that the
numbers were an artifact of unrelated suites, no longer holds: the
combined figures (92.3 / 96.0 / 93.7 \% line) are real and only 7 of 32
modules sit below 95 \%, a list short enough to work through the way the
triaged trees were (test-gap bead or category-\code{b} waiver per
point). Toggle should stay out of any triage for these trees for now:
the CPU\textquotesingle s wide
CSR/\allowbreak{}address/\allowbreak{}data buses dominate it exactly as
in the triaged trees. Promote only once the three nightly flows run
together on the same Verilator for a few cycles, so a \code{SUSPECT}
consistency row would show up before anyone triages against it.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{rtl/cpu \textbar{} mem \textbar{} gpu line
triage, slice 1 (bead \code{a5ze},
2026-10-10)}{rtl/cpu \textbar{} mem \textbar{} gpu line triage, slice 1 (bead , 2026-10-10)}}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:rtlcpu-mem-gpu-line-triage-slice-1-bead-a5ze-2026-10-10}

Scope decision (user, 2026-10-10): triage every uncovered line point of
the modules under 95 \%, write the missing tests, then move
\texttt{rtl/\allowbreak{}cpu|mem|gpu} into the line gate. \textbf{Tests
and waivers only, no RTL edits.} \textbf{Slice 1 did not flip the gate}
(\code{gpu_top} was at 88.8 \%); slice 2, below, did.

\textbf{Basis and its limit.} Measured on HEAD \code{024abfe} with
\texttt{make\ -\allowbreak{}C\ sim\ coverage\_\allowbreak{}cpu\_\allowbreak{}gpu}
(nix Verilator 5.048) before and after the new tests: \textbf{207 passed
/ 0 failed before, 222 passed / 0 failed after} (+15 = 8 + 3 + 3 + 1;
the 16th new test is the \code{expect_fail} below, which the CI grep
does not count). The SoC half is the \code{soc-coverage} artifact of the
\prref{246} run 38029069277 (RTL \code{c68f91b}), reused rather than re-run
(75 min).
\texttt{git\ diff\ c68f91b\ HEAD\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}\ rtl} is
four lines (the \code{default:} arms \prref{248} added to
\code{rv32i_pipeline_mem.sv} and \code{rv32i_pipeline_ex1c.sv}), which
\textbf{shifts the line numbers of those two files}, so the SoC
\code{.dat} cannot be unioned point-for-point with the CPU one for them
(the report\textquotesingle s consistency section flags
\code{rv32i_pipeline_ex1c} 51/69 shared). For those two modules the
table uses the CPU/GPU \code{.dat} alone (exact at HEAD, a lower bound
because the SoC firmware may add hits); every other module is the exact
SoC + CPU/GPU union. The nightly job, which runs both halves on one
HEAD, is authoritative. Note the cost of dropping the SoC half:
\code{rv32i_cpu_top}, \code{rv32i_forwarding_unit},
\code{rv32i_hazard_unit} and \code{rv32i_dcache} fall under 95 \% on the
CPU/GPU \code{.dat} alone and are above it only because of SoC hits, so
\textbf{the line gate must be evaluated on the combined data}.

\subsubsection{Remaining (slice 2) and what is not verified}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:remaining-slice-2-and-what-is-not-verified}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \code{gpu_top} register / ERROR-state paths (12 points) and a
  nested-divergence kernel that overflows the 4-deep stack at
  \code{gpu_top} level; \code{icache} \code{L498}; the open
  \code{decode} \code{L508} point.
\item
  The gate flip itself (\code{coverage_report.py} \code{TRIAGED_TREES},
  \code{soc_coverage.yml}: the combined steps lose
  \code{continue-on-error} for the part the gate depends on,
  \texttt{if:\allowbreak{}\ always()} on artifact upload, the CPU/GPU
  input becomes a hard requirement) is deliberately \textbf{not} done
  here. It lands only when every module is \textgreater= 95 \% on a
  same-HEAD combined measurement.
\item
  Not verified: a same-HEAD SoC + CPU/GPU union (the SoC half is
  c68f91b); toggle coverage was not triaged (recommendation: keep the
  100 \% control-toggle gate off these trees until the line gate has run
  a few nights); the new CPU suites were not run on the 5.036 nightly
  Verilator.
\item
  Observation, not filed: \code{rv32i_csr_file} increments no counter in
  any cycle where a legal CSR instruction is in EX (\code{:332} vs
  \code{:444}), not only the written one. The header says only the
  written register is suppressed. Whether \code{mcycle} therefore
  under-counts is \textbf{unmeasured}; slice 2 should measure it before
  deciding on a bead.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{rtl/cpu \textbar{} mem \textbar{} gpu line
triage, slice 2: remaining gaps, same-HEAD measurement, gate flip (bead
\code{a5ze},
2026-10-10)}{rtl/cpu \textbar{} mem \textbar{} gpu line triage, slice 2: remaining gaps, same-HEAD measurement, gate flip (bead , 2026-10-10)}}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:rtlcpu-mem-gpu-line-triage-slice-2-remaining-gaps-same-head-measurement-gate-flip-bead-a5ze-2026-10-10}

\textbf{Basis.} HEAD \code{b8bb221} (after \prref{257}, the \code{47lf}
warp-count fix) plus this branch. The
CPU/\allowbreak{}cache/\allowbreak{}GPU half is a full local
\texttt{make\ -\allowbreak{}C\ sim\ coverage\_\allowbreak{}cpu\_\allowbreak{}gpu}
(nix Verilator 5.048). The SoC half is the \code{soc-coverage} artifact
of the green \texttt{SoC\ Coverage} run 38045855126 on \prref{257}\textquotesingle s branch (\code{c44f5ec});
\texttt{git\ diff\ c44f5ec\ origin/\allowbreak{}main\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}\ rtl}
is \textbf{empty}, so its RTL is HEAD\textquotesingle s RTL and the
point sets agree (the report\textquotesingle s consistency section is
empty). That closes the slice-1 caveat (stale SoC half for \code{ex1c} /
\code{pipeline_mem}). One honest wrinkle: the local
\code{coverage_cpu_gpu} target stopped before its merge step because one
test (\code{test_soft_reset_recovers_from_error}) was red at that
moment, for a harness reason fixed afterwards (an \code{expect_fail}
test must fail with \code{AssertionError}, it raised
\code{TimeoutError}); I merged the 32 per-simulation \code{.dat} files
by hand with the same
\texttt{verilator\_\allowbreak{}coverage\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}write}
the target runs, and re-ran that kernel suite on its own (6/6 pass). The
nightly job runs the unmodified target end to end.

\textbf{Result of the flipped gate on that data:
\texttt{GATE\ PASS:\allowbreak{}\ 74\ gated\ modules}} (42 triaged + 32
in \texttt{rtl/\allowbreak{}cpu|mem|gpu}), 0 below the 95 \% line floor,
no consistency warning. Per tree (line \%, with waivers): \src{rtl/cpu}
99.3 (548/\allowbreak{}552), \src{rtl/mem} 97.8 (268/\allowbreak{}274),
\src{rtl/gpu} 98.7 (297/\allowbreak{}301). \textbf{Every \code{gpu_top}
point is closed or waived (100 \%).} Lowest modules, i.e. the ones with
the least headroom: \code{gpu_compute_unit} 95.5 (64/67, three
uncovered, \textbf{one more uncovered point fails the gate}),
\code{rv32i_cache_arbiter} 96.3, \code{rv32i_cpu_top} 96.9,
\code{rv32i_dcache} 97.1, \code{rv32i_hazard_unit} 97.4,
\code{memory_coalescer} 97.5.

\subsubsection{Gate: what is and is not covered}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:gate-what-is-and-is-not-covered}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Gated for line: all 32 modules of
  \texttt{rtl/\allowbreak{}cpu|mem|gpu}. No module needed a named
  exception, so no line-floor exception file exists; if one ever must (a
  module that cannot reach 95 \% without an RTL change) it gets an entry
  tied to its bead rather than a lower floor.
\item
  Not gated, deliberately: control toggle on these trees (see "Gate
  decision"). Toggle was not triaged.
\item
  \textbf{The real validation of the flip is the \texttt{SoC\ Coverage}
  job on this PR.} It touches the workflow, so the full job runs: SoC
  half, CPU/GPU half, combined report, gate. What was verified locally
  is the script (\src{tb/tests/test_coverage_report.py}, tests written
  first) and the gate result on the same-RTL data above; the workflow
  YAML itself was only parsed, not executed.
\item
  The CPU suites still run only in the nightly / this job
  (\texttt{make\ -\allowbreak{}C\ sim\ test}), not per PR; the gate is
  what makes a regression there visible.
\end{itemize}

waive only what is structurally unreachable with a reason, prune stale
waivers on measured data. Before = merged data of the main
\code{soc_coverage} run 39107233271 (RTL at 92e0243); after = sharded
run 39119364474 of \prref{316} (8 shards + CPU/GPU job, gate green, 826
tests passed / 0 failed, \code{PASS_FLOOR} 813 -\textgreater{} 823).

{

\subsubsection{The prover: waivers cite a proof, not a netlist lookup}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:the-prover-waivers-cite-a-proof-not-a-netlist-lookup}

\src{tools/verif/proven_const_bits.py} (yosys
\texttt{opt\_\allowbreak{}dff\ -\allowbreak{}sat}) cannot decide a
register-bank flop held at its reset value by
\texttt{WMASK\ =\allowbreak{}\ 0}, because the flop has no initial
value. New \src{tools/verif/stuck_bits_formal.py} closes that gap with
an unbounded model-checking proof (SymbiYosys, ABC \code{pdr}, preceded
by a short \code{bmc3} search for quick counter-examples). For a module
it takes the bits that never moved in either direction in the merged
\code{.dat}, wraps the module, assumes only that reset is asserted in
cycles 0-1 (every simulation here resets first), leaves \textbf{every
other input free}, and asserts that each bit equals its cycle-2 value
for ever after (so a combinational bit like \code{prdata[k]} must be
independent of the free inputs too). PASS = proof for all input
sequences; FAIL = a counter-example exists = a test gap (never a
waiver); timeout = \code{unknown}, reported.
\src{tools/verif/stuck_bits_waivers.py} renders the PASS bits as waiver
lines and \textbf{refuses a proven bit that lies inside a documented
writable mask} (it would be a documentation or RTL finding); none
occurred.
\texttt{-\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}tie\ PORT=\allowbreak{}VAL} holds
inputs constant (used once, below). Both tools are unit tested in
\src{tb/tests/test_stuck_bits_waivers.py}.

Waivers added (category b), about 2 770 points net (the denominator also
moved with main\textquotesingle s own RTL changes):

wrapper that selects the STUB/RNM implementation with a string

an unknown module. Adding it (and the DFT mux and memory-collar sources
\code{pll_subsystem} and \code{npu_top} instantiate) makes both
analysable; the previous "reject" was a missing file, not a frontend
limitation.

\subsubsection{Findings (no RTL change made)}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:findings-no-rtl-change-made}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{\code{dma_engine} CTRL mirror: \code{hw_wen_i[REG_CTRL]} /
  \code{hw_wdata_i[REG_CTRL]} are flops without a reset}
  (\code{dma_engine.sv:639-647} sit inside the \code{else} of the
  FSM\textquotesingle s \texttt{if\ (!rst\_\allowbreak{}n)}). They feed
  the register bank\textquotesingle s hardware-write port, which the
  bank applies on the first edge after reset is released, so in silicon
  (or X-propagating gate simulation) CTRL, including the
  \code{start}/\code{soft_reset} bits, can load power-up garbage then.
  In 2-state Verilator the flops start at 0, so no simulation can see
  it; the prover finds it because it lets the un-reset flops start
  anywhere, which is why \code{ctrl_reg[31:3]}, \code{regs_o[3][31:3]}
  and \code{hw_wdata_i[3][31:2]} (about 180 points) are \textbf{not}
  waived. Reported for the RTL owner; not worked around here.
\item
  \code{pll_subsystem} and the SoC share one decoupling fact: the
  register banks ignore \code{paddr[1:0]}; the GPU walk now proves it at
  SoC level.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{Still open}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:still-open}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \code{dma_engine}: the CTRL mirror points above, \code{dst_q[1:0]}
  (needs a descriptor with an unaligned destination: not attempted, the
  AXI side of an unaligned INCR burst is outside this PR) and
  \code{words_rem_q[31:10]} (needs a transfer of at least 4 KB words =
  16 KB; the SRAM model realises 4 KB).
\item
  \code{apb4_register_bank}: \code{addr_word[29:10]},
  \code{paddr[31:12]}, \code{hw_wen_i[13:10,31:26]} of the instance with
  a 32-bit window (not reachable from the unit wrapper).
\item
  Not in xkw5\textquotesingle s scope and untouched:
  \code{mbist_ctrl}/\code{soc_mbist}/\code{mbist_status_sync},
  \code{async_axi_fifo}/\code{cdc_gray_fifo}, \code{sram_controller},
  \code{boot_rom}, and the crossbar/\allowbreak{}ring upper-address, ID,
  burst and response fields that no master can drive
  (CPU/\allowbreak{}DMA/\allowbreak{}GPU address windows are walked now,
  IDs and burst fields are constant per master).
\item
  The SBY prover needs about 3 GB per PDR job; it runs offline (not in
  CI).
\end{itemize}

\subsection{7ovx register-walk re-measurement (2026-10-09)}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:7ovx-register-walk-re-measurement-2026-10-09}

Bead \code{7ovx} (\ghref{216} follow-up). Most of the remaining toggle gap
on the register-bank peripherals and the fabric was upper
data/\allowbreak{}address bits that never saw both edges, because tests
wrote small values to a few low offsets. New reusable helper
\src{tb/cocotb/soc/reg_walk.py} + documented maps \code{reg_maps.py}
(transcribed from the module headers and
\texttt{Doc:\allowbreak{}\ ds:\allowbreak{}mm:\allowbreak{}memory-\allowbreak{}map},
NOT read back from the RTL\textquotesingle s
\code{RESET_VAL}/\code{WMASK}) + CPU-firmware generator
\code{soc_reg_fw.py}.

\textbf{What a walk checks (behaviour, not just toggles).} Per register:
idle/\allowbreak{}reset value; walking ones (32), walking zeros (32),
\code{0xFFFFFFFF}/\code{0}/\code{0xA5A5A5A5}/\code{0x5A5A5A5A}, each
read back against
\texttt{(model\ \&\ \textasciitilde{}wmask)\ |\ (written\ \&\ wmask)} so
every RO / reserved / WO-reads-0 bit is proven to stay put; per-lane
\code{pstrb} writes and \code{pstrb=0}; PSLVERR never; restore to reset;
a second pass that every register still holds its idle value after all
the others were written (cross-talk); unmapped words read 0 and writes
to them disturb nothing (catches a snoop decoding too few address bits).
Side-effect bits are removed from the driven pattern (\code{Reg.drive})
rather than the register dropped: WDT \code{CTRL.enable}, TRNG
\code{CTRL.enable}, PMU \code{CTRL[1:0]} power-mode request,
CRYPTO/\allowbreak{}NPU \code{CTRL.START}. Skipped registers print their
reason: \code{UART_TX}, \code{SPI_TX}, \code{I2C_TX_DATA},
\code{NPU_AIN} (FIFO push), \code{CRYPTO_KEY0-3}, \code{CRYPTO_DIN0-3},
\code{NPU_WDATA} (apertures), \code{I2C_CMD}, \code{WDT_FEED}
(commands). W1C/W1P are checked semantically by \code{check_w1c} and
dedicated tests (below), because they need a pending bit created first.

\textbf{Applied at unit level} (a \code{test_register_walk} added to
each existing suite, so no new Vtop build): timer, uart, spi,
interrupt\_\allowbreak{}controller (N\_\allowbreak{}SOURCES=5), gpio,
pwm, wdt, trng, pmu, pll\_\allowbreak{}apb\_\allowbreak{}regs, i2c,
crypto, npu = 13 tests. Plus 5 semantic tests: GPIO edge-sticky W1C of
all 32 pins one at a time and level-mode \code{GPIO_IRQ_CLR} is a no-op;
PWM W1C of 4 wrap bits; WDT W1C of bark and bite; NPU \code{CTRL.START}
is W1P (reads 0) and the illegal start\textquotesingle s
\code{done}/\code{cfg_rejected} clear through
IRQ\_\allowbreak{}CLR{[}0{]}/\allowbreak{}{[}1{]}; CRYPTO the same for
\code{done}. \code{check_w1c} additionally proves a 0, a non-wired bit
and a bit with its byte strobe low clear nothing.

\textbf{Applied at SoC level} (\code{test_soc_register_walk} in
\code{test_soc_integration.py}, same Vtop as the other six): generated
RV32I firmware (no committed binary) walks 160 table entries - 104
registers across 14 peripheral windows (6 32-bit registers get the full
set and SB byte stores through every lane, the rest the short set) plus
56 unmapped words in the windows - through CPU -\textgreater{} crossbar
-\textgreater{} \code{axi4_to_axilite} -\textgreater{}
\code{axi_lite_interconnect} -\textgreater{} \code{axil_to_apb}
-\textgreater{} \code{apb_interconnect} (-\textgreater{}
\code{apb_cdc_bridge} for both PLLs). 104 927 cycles, 74 s. Result is
read back independently over the debug APB (x30 = failing checks,
x26-x29 = first failure: address, written, read, expected). SoC-level
exclusions on top of the unit ones:
\code{IRQ_STATUS}/\code{IRQ_PENDING_MASKED} are live (they mirror other
peripherals\textquotesingle{} IRQ lines that the walk itself moves).

\textbf{Result: no RTL/doc register-map mismatch found} - every walk
passed on the first run against the transcribed maps. That is a finding
only because the checks are non-vacuous (mutants below); no bead for a
map bug was needed.

\textbf{Measured delta} (before = scheduled CI run 37910784827 on
\code{main}, 2026-10-09 09:21; after = this branch\textquotesingle s
full local run; rows shown only where toggle points changed; toggle
hit/\allowbreak{}total):

Line coverage is unchanged by the walk, except the informational CPU
modules gain from the new instruction mix
(SB/\allowbreak{}XORI/\allowbreak{}BNE loops): \code{rv32i_pipeline_mem}
20/44 -\textgreater{} 27/44, \code{rv32i_decode} 27/78 -\textgreater{}
33/78, \code{rv32i_pipeline_ex1c} 11/21 -\textgreater{} 15/21.

\textbf{Why the gain is smaller than the issue\textquotesingle s "worst
pools" suggest - what is left is structural, not a missing walk.} For
every register-bank peripheral the surviving uncovered points are
\code{hw_wdata_i} / \code{hw_wen_i} (the bank\textquotesingle s
HARDWARE-write port: for RW registers it is tied to \code{regs_o}, for
status registers it only carries the few real status bits),
\code{regs_o} / \code{regs} bits that belong to reserved, WO-snoop or
RO-constant fields, \code{pready} (constant 1, zero wait states),
\code{paddr[1:0]} (the banks ignore the byte offset; a word-aligned
master never toggles it, 4 points per module) and \code{prdata} bits
above the implemented field width. A software register walk cannot reach
these. They need either waivers with the bank-structure justification
(candidate bead) or the unaligned-address probe.
\code{soc_bus}/\code{soc_top}/\code{axi4_crossbar} residue is dominated
by the upper address bits (\code{m_awaddr} 57/256, \code{m_araddr}
67/256: the 32-bit address never leaves 0x0..0x2001\_\allowbreak{}0FFF),
the 128-bit D-cache write-back/\allowbreak{}refill data lanes
(\code{m_wdata} 120/256) and the unmapped ROM write channel
(\code{bus_rom_aw*}/\code{w*}, bead \bead{ej6j}), none of which are on
the AXI-Lite -\textgreater{} APB path.

\textbf{Waiver drift fixed in passing (file
\src{tools/verif/coverage_waivers.txt}, no waiver added or loosened).}
Three FSM-\code{default:} waivers had gone stale because later RTL edits
shifted their lines, which made the report show the default arm as an
uncovered line and two stale-waiver warnings: \code{uart_controller}
\code{L591} -\textgreater{} \code{L602} (bead \bead{rqvo}), \code{pmu}
\code{L262} -\textgreater{} \code{L275} and \texttt{L294|365}
-\textgreater{} \texttt{L307|378} (bead \bead{4a7i} header edit,
including the \code{pmu.sv:} line references in the justification text),
\code{axi_lite_interconnect} \code{L328} -\textgreater{} \code{L335}.
Each target was re-read in the RTL and is still the \code{default:} arm
it was waived as. After the fix: 0 stale waivers, line 99.9 \%
(1667/\allowbreak{}1669 - the two left are \code{i2c_controller}
L617/\allowbreak{}L645, bead \bead{pnfw}), 0 modules below the 95 \%
line floor.

\textbf{Update 2026-10-10 (bead \bead{pnfw}, items 4): two I2C
\code{default:} waivers removed.} \code{i2c_bit_engine} \code{L440} (the
bit engine\textquotesingle s \code{state_e} is
\texttt{logic\ [4:\allowbreak{}0]} with 17 names, so encodings 17..31
exist; \code{test_i2c_fsm_illegal_state_recovers_to_idle} forces all 15
through the simulator handle) and \code{i2c_controller} \code{L655} (the
loopback slave\textquotesingle s \code{slv_mode_e} has all four
encodings named, so its \code{default:} is the \code{SLV_IDLE} arm, not
an upset arm; \code{test_i2c_loopback_slave_idle_fall_arm} reaches it
with a NACKed read followed by a stop-less write). Measured on the i2c +
soc\_i2c suites only (instrumented): \code{i2c_bit_engine} 114/114,
\code{i2c_controller} 84/86 (97.7 \%, same L617/L645 left), both waivers
reported stale until deleted, none stale after. The full-regression
totals above were NOT re-measured.

\textbf{ej6j function-arm waivers} & Claim checked and found
\textbf{only partly true}. Raw \code{merged.dat}: \code{worse_resp}
entry block L149 = 77 hits while the L150 elsif and both L152 arm points
are 0; \code{last_addr} L175 if/else both 0. But arms of other functions
are counted (\code{axi4_crossbar} \code{decode} L142,
\code{axi_lite_interconnect} \code{decode} L95, \code{rv32i_csr_file}
\code{apply_csr_op} L260). A 4-input micro-repro (Verilator 5.048,
\src{tools/verif/repro/verilator_return_arm.sv}) isolates it: an
if/\allowbreak{}else-if/\allowbreak{}else chain of \code{return}
statements in a function reads 0 on every arm although called with all
inputs, while the same chain assigning the function name counts. So the
waivers are kept as a \textbf{tool limitation scoped to \code{return}
arms} (the only two functions in the tree with that shape), reworded,
and narrowed to \texttt{\textasciicircum{}L(150|152)} (the report no
longer produces an \code{L155}) and \texttt{\textasciicircum{}L175}. \\

\subsection{Gate decision}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:gate-decision}

\textbf{Enforced} (user decision, 2026-10-10, bead \bead{s1cg}; it
replaces the 2026-10-05 "informational for now"). The nightly
\code{soc_coverage} job (\src{.github/workflows/soc_coverage.yml}) runs
\texttt{coverage\_\allowbreak{}report.\allowbreak{}py\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}gate}
as a real, non-\code{continue-on-error} step after the combined-report
steps; the summary and upload steps are
\texttt{if:\allowbreak{}\ always()}, so a red gate still publishes the
report, \code{merged.dat} and
\texttt{gate\_\allowbreak{}report.\allowbreak{}\{md,\allowbreak{}json\}}.
The PR regression (\code{cocotb.yml}) and its \code{PASS_FLOOR} are
untouched, and the job still fails on a red suite, a collapsed
instrumented pass count (\code{PASS_FLOOR}) or missing data as before.

\textbf{Scope (updated by bead \bead{a5ze} slice 2, 2026-10-10).} The
triaged trees (\src{rtl/soc}, \src{rtl/periph}, \src{rtl/npu}; 42
modules) get the line floor and the control-toggle floor as before.
\textbf{\src{rtl/cpu}, \src{rtl/mem} and \src{rtl/gpu} (32 modules) now
get the 95 \% LINE floor too}, evaluated on the COMBINED data: the gate
step reads both the SoC \code{merged.dat} and the
CPU/\allowbreak{}cache/\allowbreak{}GPU \code{cpu_gpu_merged.dat}, which
the same job produced at the same commit with the same Verilator. The
\textbf{100 \% control-toggle gate stays off these three trees} (their
CSR / address / data buses dominate toggle exactly as in the triaged
trees, and no ratchet was ever calibrated for them; revisit after the
line gate has run for a few nights). The slice-1 note below that the
gate "reads the SoC \code{merged.dat} only" is superseded.

What makes the extension safe rather than decorative:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  the CPU/\allowbreak{}cache/\allowbreak{}GPU step is no longer
  \code{continue-on-error}; a follow-up step applies the same log-grep
  floor as the SoC half (\code{CPU_PASS_FLOOR}, 0 failed) and requires a
  non-empty \code{cpu_gpu_merged.dat}; a red suite or missing input
  fails the job, and the summary / upload steps stay
  \texttt{if:\allowbreak{}\ always()};
\item
  \texttt{coverage\_\allowbreak{}report.\allowbreak{}py\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}gate}
  defaults \code{--line-gate-trees} to \code{rtl/cpu,rtl/mem,rtl/gpu}; a
  gate run whose data has no module of one of those trees exits
  \textbf{2 (could not evaluate)}, never 0, so feeding it only the SoC
  \code{.dat} cannot look green.
  \texttt{-\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}line-\allowbreak{}gate-\allowbreak{}trees\ ""}
  disables the extension for a SoC-only local run;
\item
  a gated module whose inputs disagree on its point set (a
  \code{SUSPECT} row in the consistency section: different Verilator or
  different source revision) is a gate failure (\code{inconsistent}):
  the union would inflate its denominator. This is exactly the
  stale-SoC-\code{.dat} situation slice 1 hit;
\item
  category-b waivers apply to these trees like any other
  (\src{tools/verif/coverage_waivers.txt}).
\end{itemize}

{\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*368/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedleft\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*204/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*408/1000\relax}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Check
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedleft
Floor
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Measured on current RTL (42 modules)
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
Line (line + branch points, after waivers), per module & \textbf{95 \%}
& worst module \code{i2c_controller} 97.65 \% (83/85); 1667/1669
overall \\
Control-signal toggle, per module, after waivers & \textbf{100 \%}, with
a 2-entry ratchet & 40 of 42 modules at exactly 100.0 \%; \code{soc_bus}
89.17 \% (107/\allowbreak{}120), \code{soc_top} 92.48 \% (295/319) \\
\end{longtable}
}

}

\textbf{What a control signal is.} A 1-bit \textbf{scalar} signal:
Verilator names its toggle object \code{sig:0->1} with no \code{[bit]}
index, whereas a vector bit, an array element or a
\texttt{logic\ [0:\allowbreak{}0]} net is \code{sig[3]:0->1}. The rule
is mechanical (\code{is_control_toggle()} in \code{coverage_report.py}:
no \code{[} before the \code{:}), so there is no signal list to maintain
and a new
\code{valid}/\code{ready}/\code{en}/\code{irq}/\code{start}/\code{done}/\code{busy}
or FSM strobe is gated the day it is written. Datapath is never gated:
buses, per-bit address/\allowbreak{}data lanes, FSM state vectors and
arrays. That also removes the register-bank residue of bead \bead{xkw5}
without a waiver (\code{hw_wdata_i}, \code{hw_wen_i}, \code{regs_o},
\code{paddr[1:0]}, \code{prdata} bits are all vectors). The remaining
structural scalars are category-\code{b} waivers with justifications:
\code{pready:1->0} (hard-wired \texttt{1'b1} in
\code{apb4_register_bank.sv:88}, 15 modules plus the two \code{soc_top}
fan-outs), \code{pll_enable:1->0} (non-clearable by design, \ghref{89}),
\code{scan_rst_ni:1->0} (DFT reset tied inactive). One consequence worth
knowing: an FSM whose state register is a vector is gated through its
line/\allowbreak{}branch points, not through toggle.

\textbf{Why 100 \% and not a lower floor.} After those waivers 40 of 42
modules measure exactly 100.0 \%, and the numbers are bit-identical
between the local run and the independent CI run \code{38015078972}
(same line, control and total-toggle counts for all 42 modules), so
there is no evidence of run-to-run noise to leave margin for. A control
signal that stops toggling in one direction is the useful regression
signal; a 95 \% floor would let a 73-point module such as
\code{crypto_accel} silently lose three of them. The cost is zero
margin: a new control signal the tests do not toggle turns the nightly
red until a test lands, a waiver is justified or a ratchet entry is
added (below). Two data points are the evidence; if a flake ever
appears, \code{--toggle-floor} is the knob and the answer is to find the
nondeterministic test, not to loosen the floor.

\textbf{The ratchet.} \src{tools/verif/coverage_toggle_ratchet.txt}
(\texttt{module\ |\ floor\ |\ bead\ |\ justification}) overrides the
toggle floor for a module whose gap is a real, bead-tracked test gap.
Two entries, each at its measured value rounded down to 0.1 (zero points
of slack, so they cannot regress):

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-8\tabcolsep)*103/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedleft\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-8\tabcolsep)*75/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedleft\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-8\tabcolsep)*93/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-8\tabcolsep)*579/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-8\tabcolsep)*131/1000\relax}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Module
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedleft
Floor
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedleft

\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright

\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright

\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\code{soc_bus} & 89.1 \% & 89.17 \% & boot-ROM write channel never
driven at SoC level (13 points) & \code{0ntg} \\
\code{soc_top} & 92.4 \% & 92.48 \% & the same 13 \code{bus_rom_*}
points plus 11 SoC-level gaps: I2C pads, \code{uart_rx_i} fall,
\code{wdt_cpu_rst_req_q} fall, cpu/gif \code{rready} fall & \code{0ntg},
\code{4pl8} \\
\end{longtable}
}

}

The ratchet never relaxes the line floor, a stale entry (module no
longer in the report) is a warning, and a module more than 2 points
above its entry prints "raise the floor". There is no tooling that makes
"only goes up" machine-enforced: the file is tracked, so lowering a
floor or adding a module is a visible diff that needs a bead and PR
review.

\textbf{A module with nothing to gate does not pass vacuously.} No line
points, no control signals and nothing waived is the failure
\code{unmeasured}. A module with no line points but control signals is
gated on those alone (pure wiring: \code{soc_bus}, \code{pll_*},
\code{async_axi_fifo}); one whose line points are all waived passes with
a note (\code{soc_addr_map_pkg}, \code{soc_periph_map_pkg}). The gate
cannot see an RTL file that produced no row at all (today
\code{axi_pkg.sv}, \code{pll_rnm.sv} and \code{trng_ro_sky130.sv}:
constants, a real-number model and a Sky130-only cell); a row that
vanished would not be noticed.

\subsubsection{How to}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:how-to}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Waive an unreachable point} (category \code{b} only; a test
  gap is a bead, never a waiver): add one line to
  \src{tools/verif/coverage_waivers.txt}
  (\texttt{module\ |\ toggle\ |\ \textasciicircum{}sig:\allowbreak{}1-\allowbreak{}>0\$\ |\ b\ |\ why,\allowbreak{}\ with\ a\ file:\allowbreak{}line}).
  The raw percentage stays in the report and a waiver that matches
  nothing is flagged. A \textbf{line} waiver is anchored on the text of
  the waived source line, not its number:
  \texttt{module\ |\ line\ |\ @"default:\allowbreak{}\ state\_\allowbreak{}q\ <=\allowbreak{}\ S\_\allowbreak{}IDLE;"\ |\ b\ |\ why}
  (comments and spacing are ignored; identical lines take
  \texttt{\#k/\allowbreak{}N}, e.g.
  \texttt{@"default:\allowbreak{}\ ;"\#2/\allowbreak{}3}; a bare anchor
  matching several lines is an error). Check it with
  \texttt{python3\ tools/\allowbreak{}verif/\allowbreak{}coverage\_\allowbreak{}report.\allowbreak{}py\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}check-\allowbreak{}waivers\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}waivers\ tools/\allowbreak{}verif/\allowbreak{}coverage\_\allowbreak{}waivers.\allowbreak{}txt}
  (no simulation needed; every PR runs it in the QA job).
\item
  \textbf{A stale line waiver fails the gate} (bead \bead{xlc4},
  \code{stale-waiver}): the waived line was edited or removed, its
  module is gone, or the number of identical lines changed. This
  replaces the old numeric
  \texttt{\textasciicircum{}L<n>\textbackslash{}b} keys, which an edit
  above the line silently un-waived (\prref{279} shifted
  \code{sram_controller.sv} by 16 lines and the main gate went red until
  \prref{290} re-keyed it). Fix: re-read the RTL, confirm the point is
  still unreachable by design, update the anchor. Old numeric entries
  convert with
  \texttt{coverage\_\allowbreak{}report.\allowbreak{}py\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}migrate-\allowbreak{}waivers\ <file>}.
  A waiver that merely matches no \emph{uncovered} point (the point is
  now covered, or the module was not measured by this \code{.dat}) stays
  a warning: it is expected on a partial run, and a new test should not
  turn the nightly red.
\item
  \textbf{Close a gap bead}: land the test, re-run \code{soc_coverage},
  then in the same PR raise the module\textquotesingle s ratchet floor
  to the new measured value, or delete the entry once the module reaches
  100 \%.
\item
  \textbf{Add a ratchet entry} (a deliberate relaxation): needs a bead
  and a justification in the file; use it only for a real, tracked test
  gap.
\item
  \textbf{Re-evaluate without re-simulating}:
  \texttt{python3\ tools/\allowbreak{}verif/\allowbreak{}coverage\_\allowbreak{}report.\allowbreak{}py\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}dat\ <merged.\allowbreak{}dat>\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}waivers\ tools/\allowbreak{}verif/\allowbreak{}coverage\_\allowbreak{}waivers.\allowbreak{}txt\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}gate\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}toggle-\allowbreak{}ratchet\ tools/\allowbreak{}verif/\allowbreak{}coverage\_\allowbreak{}toggle\_\allowbreak{}ratchet.\allowbreak{}txt}.
  Exit 0 pass, 1 a module is under a floor (the failing list is
  printed), 2 the gate could not be evaluated (bad or empty data, bad
  waiver or ratchet file). Without \code{--gate} the script exits 0 for
  any coverage level, exactly as before.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Reproducing}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:reproducing}

\begin{lstlisting}[language=bash]
python3 -m pytest tb/tests/test_coverage_report.py          # report-script tests
nix develop --command make -C tb/cocotb/soc soc_coverage SIM_BUILD_ROOT=/nobackup/claude_sim_build/soc_cov
python3 tools/verif/coverage_report.py --dat <merged.dat> --waivers tools/verif/coverage_waivers.txt \
    --out-md report.md --out-json report.json              # re-render without re-simulating
\end{lstlisting}
\end{relocated}

\chapter{CDC and lint checks}\label{tm:ch:cdc-and-lint-checks}

This chapter describes the CDC checks and the lint checks. It lists the waivers.

\begin{relocated}{docs/verification/CDC_SNITCH_POC.md}
% Converted from docs/verification/CDC_SNITCH_POC.md by tools/docs/md2tex.py; edit this file, not the original.
\section{CDC Snitch integration --- feasibility POC}\label{tm:flow:cdc-snitch-poc}

\textbf{Status:} parked / informational (2026-06-09) \textbf{Tool:}
\href{https://github.com/BerkeleyLab/Bedrock/blob/master/build-tools/cdc_snitch.md}{BerkeleyLab/\allowbreak{}Bedrock
\code{cdc_snitch}} (pinned commit \code{8929d4d}) \textbf{Runner:}
\texttt{make\ -\allowbreak{}C\ sim\ cdc} → \src{tools/cdc/} (see
\src{tools/cdc/README.md})

\subsection{Goal}\label{tm:flow:cdc-snitch-poc:goal}

Evaluate whether Berkeley Lab\textquotesingle s open-source
\code{cdc_snitch} can detect clock-domain-crossing (CDC) issues in this
RV32I + GPU-lite SoC and serve as a CI gate.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{What cdc\_\allowbreak{}snitch
does}{What cdc\_snitch does}}\label{tm:flow:cdc-snitch-poc:what-cdc-snitch-does}

A small Python tool that runs Verilog through \textbf{yosys} (≥0.23),
flattens the design to gates/\allowbreak{}memories, writes a JSON
netlist, and classifies every register by whether its data-source clock
domains match its own clock:

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-2\tabcolsep)*136/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-2\tabcolsep)*844/1000\relax}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Category
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Meaning
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\code{OK1} & all inputs in the register\textquotesingle s own domain
(normal logic) \\
\code{CDC} & single-source crossing through trivial logic, marked
\code{magic_cdc} (intentional) \\
\code{OKX} & single-source crossing, not marked (e.g. an input-sampling
flop) \\
\code{BAD} & a register\textquotesingle s data combines
\textbf{multiple} clock domains (the dangerous anti-pattern) \\
\end{longtable}
}

Non-zero \code{BAD} → non-zero exit code. \textbf{Every top-level input
port is treated as its own clock domain.}

\subsection{Toolchain (what actually worked)}\label{tm:flow:cdc-snitch-poc:toolchain-what-actually-worked}

\begin{lstlisting}[language={}]
sv2v (SystemVerilog -> Verilog-2005)  ->  yosys (flatten -> JSON)  ->  cdc_snitch.py
\end{lstlisting}

Front-end finding: \textbf{pure oss-cad-suite could not parse this RTL.}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  yosys built-in \texttt{read\_\allowbreak{}verilog\ -\allowbreak{}sv} →
  syntax error on package-import-in-package
  (\code{soc_addr_map_pkg.sv}).
\item
  the bundled \code{slang} plugin → rejects the \textbf{frozen} Phase 3
  cache RTL\textquotesingle s mixed blocking/\allowbreak{}non-blocking
  writes to \code{valid_array}/\code{dirty_array}
  (\code{rv32i_dcache.sv}).
\end{itemize}

Adding \textbf{sv2v} as a preprocessor resolves both with \textbf{zero
RTL changes}. (Two unrelated nits were worked around in the flow:
\code{axi_lite_register_bank.sv} is absent from the Verilator
\code{SOC_TOP_SOURCES} list because Verilator auto-discovers it --- it
is added back explicitly for the CDC flow.)

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{Result (full \code{soc_top}, 52 RTL files,
\textasciitilde32k
registers)}{Result (full , 52 RTL files, \textasciitilde32k registers)}}\label{tm:flow:cdc-snitch-poc:result-full-soc-top-52-rtl-files-32k-registers}

\begin{lstlisting}[language={}]
OK1: 18249   CDC: 0   OKX: 8653   BAD: 5234
\end{lstlisting}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \ok{} The toolchain runs end-to-end and elaborates the \textbf{entire}
  SoC to a netlist.
\item
  \ok{} It \textbf{correctly recognized the real async input}
  \code{uart_rx_i}: 0 \code{BAD}, classified \code{OKX} (the 2-FF
  synchronizer in \code{uart_controller.sv} is seen). So it \emph{would}
  catch a \emph{missing} synchronizer. (Historical, at the time of the
  PoC. Since bead \bead{xtaa} the UART RX synchroniser is a
  \code{cdc_sync} instance, so the crossing is classified \code{CDC},
  and \src{tools/cdc/cdc_config.yml} \code{required_synchronisers} gates
  it explicitly.)
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{The 5234 \code{BAD} are
\textasciitilde100\% false
positives}{The 5234  are \textasciitilde100\% false positives}}\label{tm:flow:cdc-snitch-poc:the-5234-bad-are-100-false-positives}

Because cdc\_\allowbreak{}snitch models every top-level input port as a
separate clock domain, and this SoC is \textbf{single-clock}
(\code{soc_top} fans one \code{clk_i}/\code{rst_n_i} pair to
everything):

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*288/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*160/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*533/1000\relax}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Foreign "domain" flagged
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
BAD registers
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Reality
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\code{rst_n_i} (async reset) & \textasciitilde4997 (95\%) & reset, not a
clock domain \\
\code{apb_paddr_i} + APB ctrl & \textasciitilde235 & APB debug bus,
synchronous to \code{clk_i} \\
\code{spi_miso_i} & 2 & SPI MISO sampled in \code{clk_i} domain (master
mode) \\
\end{longtable}
}

There are \textbf{zero genuine internal multi-clock crossings} ---
correct, because there is only one clock. The one mildly interesting hit
is \code{u_spi.rx_shift_q} / \code{rx_push_data} sampling
\code{spi_miso_i} without a 2-FF synchronizer; harmless in master mode
but worth tracking as a hardening opportunity (to be filed in beads ---
see "Follow-ups" below).

\subsection{Follow-ups}\label{tm:flow:cdc-snitch-poc:follow-ups}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{SPI MISO hardening:} evaluate adding a 2-FF synchronizer on
  \code{spi_miso_i} in \src{rtl/periph/spi_controller.sv}
  (\code{u_spi.rx_shift_q} / \code{rx_push_data}). Not a functional bug
  in master mode, but good CDC hygiene. File as a beads issue
  (\texttt{bd\ create}).
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Decision}\label{tm:flow:cdc-snitch-poc:decision}

\textbf{Park as a documented, informational tool.} Committed:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \src{tools/cdc/} --- pinned fetch (\code{fetch_cdc_tools.sh},
  \code{versions.env}) + runner (\code{run_cdc.sh}).
\item
  \texttt{make\ -\allowbreak{}C\ sim\ cdc} --- informational by default;
  \texttt{make\ cdc\ CDC\_\allowbreak{}STRICT=\allowbreak{}1} gives gate
  semantics.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{No CI gate yet.} As-is it is a permanently-red gate drowning in
false positives.

\subsection{To make it a real gate (deferred to Phase 6+)}\label{tm:flow:cdc-snitch-poc:to-make-it-a-real-gate-deferred-to-phase-6}

When real multiple clock domains are introduced, add a thin CDC
verification wrapper that:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  neutralizes the async-reset domain (synchronize reset internally /
  exclude \code{rst_n_i}),
\item
  declares the genuinely-synchronous top-level inputs (APB debug, SPI)
  as \code{clk_i}-domain (register them once before use, since
  cdc\_\allowbreak{}snitch has no port-domain allowlist),
\item
  marks intentional crossings with the \code{magic_cdc} attribute (→
  \code{CDC} instead of \code{OKX}).
\end{enumerate}

The residual output then becomes meaningful and \code{BAD=0} can gate
CI.
\end{relocated}

Silent forms (no distinct message at all): a constant out-of-range array
index (\code{m[6]} on a 4-entry array, p04) yields

\code{struct.member[hi:lo]} part-selects are mis-offset by Synlig in ANY
expression (p10, plain \code{assign}), not only in port connections.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{sv2v function temporaries on the SoC (bead
\code{j1b7})}{sv2v function temporaries on the SoC (bead )}}\label{tm:flow:synth-undef-gate:sv2v-function-temporaries-on-the-soc-bead-j1b7}

The gate was designed and validated on the CPU macro. On the Sky130 SoC
(sv2v frontend) the structural \code{x-driven-net} rule reported
\textbf{509} hits, all of them wires named
\texttt{<func>\$func\$<file>:\allowbreak{}<line>\$<n>.\allowbreak{}<variable>}
(functions \code{decode}, \code{sbox}, \code{xtime}, \code{mix_col},
\code{key_step}, \code{strb_expand}, SHA \code{small_sigma*},
\code{sv2v_cast_*}, ...), 0 \code{x-driven-instance-input}, 0
\code{undef-range-select}. The SoC flow aborted at step 3 and needed
\code{CVT_SYNTH_GATE=warn}. The warn escape hatch is \textbf{not} part
of any recipe, doc or CI step any more; the gate runs blocking.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{Mechanism (read from the RTLIL,
\code{tb/tests/fixtures/synth_undef/functemp_*})}{Mechanism (read from the RTLIL, )}}\label{tm:flow:synth-undef-gate:mechanism-read-from-the-rtlil-tbtestsfixturessynth-undeffunctemp}

yosys inlines each function call. Per call it creates two sets of wires:
the call-site \texttt{\$result} (really driven) and the function
body\textquotesingle s own argument, local and \texttt{\$result} wires.
The \code{always}-style process that implements the call carries
\texttt{sync\ always\ /\allowbreak{}\ update\ <body\ wire>\ 4'x} for
every body wire: the body wires are \emph{declared storage} whose
default value is x, and the real value travels through proc temporaries
(\texttt{\$0\textbackslash{}<wire>\$n} /
\texttt{\$1\textbackslash{}<wire>\$n}) into the call-site result. After
\code{proc}, \texttt{connect\ \textbackslash{}<body\ wire>\ 4'x}
remains, so the JSON header shows a named net that is entirely constant
x. A \textbf{read} of such a wire (a path that never assigned the
variable) is a different object: proc resolves it into a literal
\code{x} on a \texttt{\$mux} data input. A function whose result is
unassigned on some path therefore produces the same x-named net
\emph{plus} a reader, which is the case a pure name skip would hide.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{The rule
\code{func-temp-dead-init}}{The rule }}\label{tm:flow:synth-undef-gate:the-rule-func-temp-dead-init}

A net \texttt{<func>\$func\$.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}}
that is entirely x is \textbf{exempt} only if all hold, judged on the
PRE-\code{proc} RTLIL of the same elaboration (\code{write_rtlil} after
\code{hierarchy}, passed as \code{--pre-rtlil}):

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  every x-initialised wire of the group (module base name, function,
  \code{file:line}, variable) has only full-width
  \texttt{update/\allowbreak{}assign\ <wire>\ <const\ x>};
\item
  no other driver (no non-x update/\allowbreak{}assign, no module-level
  \texttt{connect\ <wire>\ .\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}});
\item
  \textbf{no reader}: the wire appears in no cell connection, process
  right-hand side, \code{switch}, \code{sync} or \code{connect}
  right-hand side;
\item
  the group size in the proof equals the number of such nets in the JSON
  header (a proof from another elaboration exempts nothing).
\end{enumerate}

Anything else keeps \code{x-driven-net} FAIL and the text says why
(\texttt{function\ temporary\ is\ READ\ before\ it\ is\ assigned:\allowbreak{}\ <RTLIL\ line>},
\texttt{no\ pre-\allowbreak{}proc\ RTLIL\ proof\ supplied},
\texttt{group\ not\ found}, ...). No \code{--pre-rtlil} means no
exemption (fail closed). Partially-x nets, instance inputs tied to x,
log-level undef warnings and every other rule are untouched. Matching is
by names that do not depend on yosys\textquotesingle{} autoidx or on
directories, so the flow\textquotesingle s JSON (absolute paths) and the
proof run agree.

Why not "check later in the yosys script": after
\texttt{opt\_\allowbreak{}clean\ -\allowbreak{}purge} the dead wires
disappear without any name rule, but so does every unread-by-then named
x net, and a function read of x becomes an anonymous \texttt{\$mux}
input that is indistinguishable from an intentional
\texttt{default:\allowbreak{}\ y\ =\allowbreak{}\ 'x}. The pre-proc read
test is the narrowest point at which the two cases still differ.

\subsubsection{Report}\label{tm:flow:synth-undef-gate:report}

\code{check_synth_undef.py} always prints one line per run, and the
LibreLane report \code{synth_undef_gate.rpt} carries the same line:

\begin{lstlisting}[language={}]
EXEMPT  [func-temp-dead-init] <N> net(s) in <M> function(s) (constant-x update proven unread in the pre-proc RTLIL): decode=188 sbox=40 ...
\end{lstlisting}

\code{-v} lists every exempted net. \code{--json} has
\code{exempt_by_function} and \code{exempt_total}. A \textbf{new
function} shows up as a new \code{name=count} entry; a function whose
temporaries get read shows up as \code{x-driven-net} FAIL instead.
Nothing needs to be added anywhere to introduce a function: if its
temporaries are never read it is exempt automatically; if they are read,
fix the RTL (assign the result on every path).

Sky130 SoC exemptions by function:
\texttt{decode=\allowbreak{}188\ sbox=\allowbreak{}40\ xtime=\allowbreak{}32\ sv2v\_\allowbreak{}cast\_\allowbreak{}16=\allowbreak{}32\ sv2v\_\allowbreak{}cast\_\allowbreak{}16\_\allowbreak{}signed=\allowbreak{}32\ sv2v\_\allowbreak{}cast\_\allowbreak{}18=\allowbreak{}32\ mix\_\allowbreak{}col=\allowbreak{}28\ strb\_\allowbreak{}expand=\allowbreak{}18\ sv2v\_\allowbreak{}cast\_\allowbreak{}C850C=\allowbreak{}16\ sv2v\_\allowbreak{}cast\_\allowbreak{}8F8A1=\allowbreak{}12\ key\_\allowbreak{}step=\allowbreak{}11\ sv2v\_\allowbreak{}cast\_\allowbreak{}32=\allowbreak{}10\ min2=\allowbreak{}8\ round\_\allowbreak{}tail=\allowbreak{}5\ ch=\allowbreak{}4\ maj=\allowbreak{}4\ sv2v\_\allowbreak{}cast\_\allowbreak{}10D7B=\allowbreak{}4\ sv2v\_\allowbreak{}cast\_\allowbreak{}8=\allowbreak{}4\ sv2v\_\allowbreak{}cast\_\allowbreak{}C2B14=\allowbreak{}4\ sub\_\allowbreak{}bytes=\allowbreak{}3\ big\_\allowbreak{}sigma0/\allowbreak{}1=\allowbreak{}2\ each\ sha\_\allowbreak{}k=\allowbreak{}2\ shift\_\allowbreak{}rows=\allowbreak{}2\ small\_\allowbreak{}sigma0/\allowbreak{}1=\allowbreak{}2\ each\ sub\_\allowbreak{}word=\allowbreak{}2\ mix\_\allowbreak{}columns=\allowbreak{}2\ sv2v\_\allowbreak{}cast\_\allowbreak{}0E339=\allowbreak{}2\ sv2v\_\allowbreak{}cast\_\allowbreak{}3=\allowbreak{}2\ sv2v\_\allowbreak{}cast\_\allowbreak{}9=\allowbreak{}2}
(509 = the 509 of the bead, 0 left).

\textbf{x never reaches anything observable.} For every exempted group
the proof requires that no read of the wire can reach a cell input, a
\code{switch}/\code{sync}, a process update of a real signal or an
output port. Besides outright unread wires this covers the \emph{hold
read}: a call made inside an \code{if} of a combinational block makes
\code{proc} re-assign the function\textquotesingle s own wire to itself
on the other branch (a read), but that value only feeds the dead
\texttt{\$0} temporaries of x-initialised wires. The proof follows each
read through the process assignments (flow-insensitive, so
conservative), which is why \code{sv2v_cast_*} calls in
\code{axi4_crossbar} are exempt while \code{leaky} below is not.
Independently, the structural rule \code{x-driven-instance-input}
reports 0 hits on the SoC (an x on any instance input would be a FAIL).
Measured on the same Sky130 SoC elaboration (all-x function-temp nets /
x on user-instance inputs): after \code{proc} 509 / 0, after
\code{opt_clean} 277 / 0, after
\texttt{opt\_\allowbreak{}clean\ -\allowbreak{}purge} 0 / 0 - the wires
vanish by themselves once nothing reads them, with no name rule. Flow
path: the LibreLane plugin (blocking, no \code{CVT_SYNTH_GATE}) was run
to \code{Yosys.JsonHeader} for both Sky130 SoC configs:
\texttt{verdict=\allowbreak{}PASS\ fail=\allowbreak{}0}, 509 exempt, one
extra elaboration for the proof, 24 s, 380 MB peak for the whole
LibreLane process.

The struct-member form of the dud4 bug is Synlig-specific (sv2v flattens
members to in-range slices), so control (a1) uses the plain out-of-range
select, which yosys turns into the same
\texttt{Range\ select\ .\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}\ out\ of\ bounds\ .\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}\ Setting\ all\ N\ result\ bits\ to\ undef}
substitution. Snippets: \src{tools/verif/synth_gate/inject/}. Unit
tests: \src{tb/tests/test_check_synth_undef_functemp.py} (35 tests,
real-yosys fixtures
\texttt{tb/\allowbreak{}tests/\allowbreak{}fixtures/\allowbreak{}synth\_\allowbreak{}undef/\allowbreak{}functemp\_\allowbreak{}\{clean,\allowbreak{}unassigned,\allowbreak{}hold\}.\allowbreak{}*}).

\subsubsection{Flow wiring}\label{tm:flow:synth-undef-gate:flow-wiring}

The LibreLane plugin writes the proof lazily: only if the JSON header
contains x-driven nets that look like function temporaries and the
frontend is not Synlig, it re-elaborates the step\textquotesingle s own
sources (same config, defines and top) with plain yosys up to
\code{hierarchy} and dumps \code{func_temp_proof.pre.il} into the
JsonHeader step directory (one extra elaboration, see Measured). If that
fails the step fails ("never a pass").

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{Post-\allowbreak{}incrementation\ operations\ are\ handled\ as\ pre-\allowbreak{}incrementation}}{}}\label{tm:flow:synth-undef-gate:post-incrementation-operations-are-handled-as-pre-incrementation}

The "30" warnings in the dud4 logs are 15 source sites, each reported by
both the JSON-header and synthesis logs: \code{rv32i_regfile.sv:46},
\code{rv32i_dcache.sv:250,253,499,510,547,952},
\code{rv32i_icache.sv:203,206,412,423,453,517,605},
\code{soc_addr_map_pkg.sv:72}. \textbf{All 15 are
\texttt{for\ (.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{};\ i+\allowbreak{}+\allowbreak{})}
loop steps whose value is unused.} Probe results (miter + SAT, Synlig vs
sv2v elaboration of the same source): loop-step \code{i++} (p14, p20)
proven equivalent. When the value IS used
(\texttt{z[j+\allowbreak{}+\allowbreak{}]\ =\allowbreak{}\ .\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}},
\texttt{y\ =\allowbreak{}\ j+\allowbreak{}+\allowbreak{}}; p13, p19)
Synlig does not miscompile silently: it stops with
\texttt{ERROR:\allowbreak{}\ Don't\ know\ how\ to\ detect\ sign\ and\ width\ for\ AST\_\allowbreak{}ASSIGN\_\allowbreak{}EQ\ node!}.
So the class cannot produce a second silent miscompile in this repo as
it stands, and no bead was filed. The rule stays WARN and is not
allowlisted. The same miter flow flags p01 and p10 as NOT equivalent
(negative control of the harness).

\subsection{Allowlist}\label{tm:flow:synth-undef-gate:allowlist}

\src{tools/verif/synth_undef_allowlist.txt}: format
\texttt{rule\ |\ regex\ |\ justification\ (>=\allowbreak{}\ 20\ chars)}.
Current entries: the \code{implicit-decl} warnings for the initialisers
of the dead \code{_unused_csr_wr_en} / \code{_unused_csr_illegal} /
\code{_unused_*} lint sinks (read by nothing). \code{post-increment},
\code{removed-module}, \code{mem2reg} need no entry (not FAIL). Stale
entries are reported with \code{-v}.

\subsection{What the gate does not cover}\label{tm:flow:synth-undef-gate:what-the-gate-does-not-cover}

Messages the tools never print; defects that leave no \code{x} at the
elaboration boundary (for example a mis-elaborated runtime mux, the
\code{OPT_MUXTREE} class of beads gcd/b0t); defects introduced after
elaboration (ABC, resizer); anything in a flow that does not go through
\src{pnr/Makefile}. It is a tripwire for the undef-substitution class,
not a proof of frontend correctness; the gate-vs-RTL differential
(\src{tools/verif/gls/}) remains the functional proof.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{CI job
(\src{.github/workflows/synth-undef-gate.yml},
\src{tools/verif/synth_gate/ci_smoke.sh})}{CI job (, )}}\label{tm:flow:synth-undef-gate:ci-job-githubworkflowssynth-undef-gateyml-toolsverifsynth-gateci-smokesh}

Path-filtered on \code{rtl/**}, \code{pnr/**}, \code{tools/verif/**},
the gate tests and fixtures. Uses the pinned oss-cad-suite yosys + sv2v
of the CDC job (same cache key) and the pinned nix Verilator.
\code{ci_smoke.sh} drives \src{tools/verif/synth_gate/elab_gate.sh}
(source list and defines from
\texttt{make\ -\allowbreak{}C\ pnr\ <target>-\allowbreak{}sv2v-\allowbreak{}inputs},
never a generated file; sv2v/\allowbreak{}yosys errors are printed).
Steps: (1) \textbf{positive controls, blocking mode}: Sky130 CPU macro,
Sky130 SoC and ASAP7 SoC must PASS; (2) \textbf{negative controls}, each
must FAIL for the stated reason: CPU \code{u_hazard} out-of-range select
(the dud4 lines themselves cannot be the control, sv2v elaborates them
correctly, which is why the PD flow uses sv2v), and in SoC scope an
out-of-range struct-member select, an instance port tied to \texttt{'x},
and a function result left unassigned on a path (injected into a scratch
copy of \code{timer.sv}, reaching \code{irq_o}; snippets in
\src{tools/verif/synth_gate/inject/}); (3)
\texttt{synth\ -\allowbreak{}flatten} + \code{techmap} +
\texttt{abc\ -\allowbreak{}g} to yosys generic gates (no PDK) and a
Verilator gate-vs-RTL differential on \code{trivial},
\code{ma7_straight}, \code{ma7_branch} (commit stream, APB debug reads,
AXI writes must be identical). Runtime and peak RSS on the GitHub runner
(\prref{271}, all green): whole job 2 m 13 s; \code{ci_smoke.sh} 1 m 36 s
wall, 426 MB peak (the Verilator build of step 3); gate cases: Sky130
CPU 2 s / 61 MB, Sky130 SoC 4 s / 93 MB, ASAP7 SoC 22 s / 154 MB, each
negative control 2-5 s / 61-95 MB. The job timeout is 20 min.

Does NOT cover: the Synlig frontend (oss-cad-suite has no Synlig plugin;
the Synlig arm is protected only by the PD-time plugin); GPU macros; PDK
cell models and ABC mapping with the real liberty; timing. The
differential\textquotesingle s own negative control was not re-run for
this job (it was exercised in dud4 with the Synlig netlist, which
diverges). First CI run on GitHub is pending.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{Netlist census tripwire
(\src{tools/verif/check_cpu_netlist_census.py})}{Netlist census tripwire ()}}\label{tm:flow:synth-undef-gate:netlist-census-tripwire-toolsverifcheck-cpu-netlist-censuspy}

After a Sky130 \code{rv32i_cpu_top} synthesis the plugin requires the
\code{fwd_b_sel_r}, \code{fwd_b_ex1c_r}, \code{fwd_b_ex1b2_r} nets and
5220-5370 flops (re-centred from 4800-4950 on 2026-10-18, bead
\bead{c54a}: the in-macro \code{mbist_ctrl} adds 406 flops standalone,
the Sky130 CPU synthesis went 4868 -\textgreater{} 5286; the fwd\_b\_*
net requirement is unchanged and is the real tripwire). Measured on the
dud4 netlists: sv2v main 4868 flops + all three nets (PASS); Synlig main
4863 flops, nets missing (FIRES); pinned Synlig 4829 flops, nets missing
(FIRES). It is a tripwire for this one known casualty, not a proof.
ASAP7 netlists do not keep these net names.
\end{relocated}

\chapter{Proprietary simulators}\label{tm:ch:proprietary-simulators}

This chapter describes how to run the proprietary simulators.
